# DEVLOG

Internal development log for the repository.

## Tracking files

The repository tracks its work in four Markdown files:

- `CHANGELOG.md`: user-facing changes per release.
- `DEVLOG.md` (this file): internal log of completed technical work, with day-to-day notes (tests, decisions, blockers).
- `TODO.md`: active short-term tasks and priorities.
- `ROADMAP.md`: medium- and long-term initiatives.

Recommended flow: track active work in `TODO.md`, move completed outcomes to `DEVLOG.md`, and promote user-facing changes to `CHANGELOG.md` during release preparation.

## Conventions

- Add one entry per work block or PR.
- Use status: `done`, `in progress`, or `blocked`.
- Reference touched file paths, and issues and pull requests when available, in the format of the conventions of `TODO.md` (`#123` for this repository, `owner/repo#123` for others).
- Include a minimal verification step (tests, script, or manual check).
- Keep entries in reverse chronological order (most recent first).

## Entry template

```md
### YYYY-MM-DD
#### [status] Short title
- Context:
- Changes:
- Files:
- Verification:
- Next step:
```

## Entries

### 2026-11-11
#### [done] A3: the hook of the SET with the dynamic clothing model adds the Pierce model (self-audit of session 2) (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: finding A3 (bug) of the self-audit of session 2: `apply_set_setpoints(..., clo=None, method='bisection')` on a model whose People objects have the dynamic clothing model (`DynamicClothingModelASHRAE55` or `CalculationMethodSchedule`) added the sensors `SET_CloValue_<zone>` of *Zone Thermal Comfort Clothing Value*, which EnergyPlus sets up only for the People objects with the Fanger, Pierce or KSU model (ThermalComfort.cc of EnergyPlus 25.1); the models of the ACCIS with temperature setpoints have AdaptiveASH55 and AdaptiveCEN15251 only, so EnergyPlus stopped before the simulation in both backends. `apply_sun2026_setpoints` already added the Pierce model.
- Changes: `accim.sim.comfort_hooks.apply_set_setpoints`: with `clo=None` and the dynamic clothing model in a zone, the People objects get the Pierce model (`add_thermal_comfort_model`, after `_people_inputs(..., pierce=True)` checks the schedules that it needs, before changing anything) unless they all have the Fanger, Pierce or KSU model (`_needs_comfort_model`, `_CLOTHING_VALUE_MODELS`; the People:Definition objects of EnergyPlus 26.2 too). The models that ran before do not change. Docstring, section 4.9.2 of the detailed use, the SET entry of `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Files: `accim/sim/comfort_hooks.py`, `tests/core/test_sun2026_set.py`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` (§43), `DEVLOG.md`.
- Verification: `test_the_hook_of_the_set_with_the_dynamic_clothing_model_adds_the_pierce_model` (the People objects get Pierce, not with Fanger already nor with the clothing insulation given; the `ValueError` without the work efficiency schedule, the model unchanged) and `test_the_model_of_the_hook_of_the_set_with_the_dynamic_clothing_model_runs` (EnergyPlus 25.1, the EMS backend without the hook, 15 July) fail without the fix (EnergyPlus: "FATAL: Errors found in processing Energy Management System input") and pass with it. `tests/core/test_sun2026_set.py` 9 passed, 8 skipped, 4 failed and `tests/core/test_comfort_hooks.py` 17 passed, 2 skipped, 14 failed, all failures the `ImportError` of numba, as before. Compiles with Python 3.9.

### 2026-11-11
#### [done] A7: the saturated hours of the hook of the ATHB are those of the run period (self-audit of session 2) (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: finding A7 (bug) of the self-audit of session 2 (`notes/attachments/autoauditoria_sesion2_v1.2.0.json`): `AthbSetpointHook` added up its saturated hours "out of the warm-up days" with `controller.get('WarmupFlag')`, but `ErlController` updates the built-in variables only for the EMS programs that read them, and no program of a model of the ACCIS without the outdoor metric of the custom model or the limiter of the drift reads `WarmupFlag`, so it was `None` and the hours of the warm-up days and of the sizing periods (design days) were added up too (the audit measured 4476 warm-up calls and 576 calls of design days out of their warm-up in a 2-day run).
- Changes: `accim.sim.comfort_hooks._in_run_period(controller)`: the timesteps of the run periods of the weather file (`kind_of_sim` of the API of EnergyPlus is 3, `_RUN_PERIOD_WEATHER`) out of their warm-up days (`warmup_flag`); with a controller without the API, `WarmupFlag` as before. `AthbSetpointHook.__call__` uses it for `saturated_hours`; its docstring, section 4.9.1 of the detailed use and the ATHB entry of `CHANGELOG.md`. The outputs `ATHB_saturated_<zone>` do not change.
- Files: `accim/sim/comfort_hooks.py`, `tests/core/test_comfort_hooks.py`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` (§43), `DEVLOG.md`.
- Verification: `test_the_saturated_hours_are_those_of_the_run_period_out_of_the_warm_up_days` (a fake controller whose API gives the kind of simulation and the warm-up flag, without the variable `WarmupFlag`) fails without the fix (it counted the warm-up: 1/6 h instead of 0) and passes with it; `test_the_hooks_count_the_hours_of_the_run_period_only` (EnergyPlus 25.1, the real controller, 15–16 July of the sample model, a probe hook in the process of the simulation) counts exactly the 288 timesteps of the run period, all with `kind_of_sim` 3 and out of the warm-up, and leaves out the design days and the warm-up days. `tests/core/test_comfort_hooks.py`: 17 passed, 2 skipped, 14 failed, all with the `ImportError` of numba (Smart App Control), as before. Compiles with Python 3.9.

### 2026-11-11
#### [done] S3: the reference of the rate of the limiter of the drift, the mean of the occupied timesteps of the previous day (decision 13) (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: decision 13 (`notes/decisions.md`, the verdicts of 2026-11-11; the handoff of Claude Science §3, #13): with `drift_mode='rate'`, the reference of the daily limit is the mean of the setpoints applied in the occupied timesteps of the previous day, not that of its last timestep (the provisional option (i) of F2); the days without occupied timesteps keep the reference of the last occupied day (decision 14). The same for setpoints constant within the occupied timesteps of a day (the ACCIS without hooks); different for those of the hooks, which follow the humidity and the mean radiant temperature.
- Changes: `accim.sim.ems.drift_limiter.program_lines`: in the occupied timesteps outside the warm-up, after the clamps, `DriftSumAHST_<zone>` and `DriftSumACST_<zone>` add the applied setpoints (counted by `n_occ_<zone>`, the counter of the operative temperature of T_rm_in); at the change of `DayOfYear`, if `n_occ_<zone>` > 0, `DriftRefAHST_<zone>`/`DriftRefACST_<zone>` are the sums divided by it; the sums go back to 0 with the others; `DriftLastAHST_<zone>`/`DriftLastACST_<zone>` removed (`_STATE`, so `InitDriftLimiter` too). The docstrings of the module and of `AddAccis` (`drift_mode`), section 4.2.7 of the detailed use, `CHANGELOG.md` (the entry of the limiter), `notes/decisions.md` (the implementation in the F2 record).
- Files: `accim/sim/ems/drift_limiter.py`, `accim/sim/accis.py`, `tests/core/test_drift_limiter.py`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` (§40), `notes/decisions.md`, `DEVLOG.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
- Verification: `tests/core/test_drift_limiter.py` 12 passed and 1 failed only because `pythermalcomfort.models` cannot be imported on this server (numba blocked by Smart App Control; the test calls `apply_set_setpoints`); `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_drift_parameters.py` 2 passed. New: with the Erl interpreter, a hook whose setpoints rise 0.25 K per hour gives the mean of the occupied hours (23.125 °C), not the last one (25.75 °C); the test of the day without occupants follows decision 14 (the reference of the last occupied day); with EnergyPlus 25.1, from Monday 1 to Tuesday 9 April, the same rising setpoints given by a Python hook (API backend) and by the equivalent Erl lines at the beginning of `RunACCIS_<zone>` (EMS backend) give identical results (`check_exact`), with the rule of the mean checked day by day with pandas (also the weekend without occupants) and the limit active on Tuesday; the test of the two backends checks that the reference is the mean of the applied setpoints of the occupied timesteps, and equal to that of the last timestep with the setpoints of the ACCIS (constant in the occupied timesteps of each day of the sample office). This also covers finding A10 of the self-audit (the rate never active in an EnergyPlus test). The changed modules compile with Python 3.9.
- Next step: S4.

### 2026-11-11
#### [done] S1: the warning of the minimum and maximum of the setpoints, with the setpoints of the comfort model (decision 19) (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: decision 19 (`notes/decisions.md`, the verdicts of 2026-11-11; the handoff of Claude Science §2.2): the warning of verdict 13 of v04, implemented literally in G5, compared the minimum heating setpoint and the maximum cooling setpoint with `ACSTaul`/`AHSTall`, applicability limits of the outdoor metric (10 and 30 °C for INT EN16798), not setpoints; task S1 of `notes/COPILOT_SIGUIENTE_SESION.md` replaces it by option (ii).
- Changes: `accim.sim.ems.setpoint_limits.warn_crossed_setpoint_limits(idf, epw=None)` (the comparison with the applicability limits, `_applicability_limits`, removed; unreleased, so no deprecation): without `epw`, no warning; with it, for each zone with a minimum or a maximum, the setpoints of its comfort model from `accim.sim.preview.compute_setpoints` (the arguments of its `SetInputData_<zone>` without the limits, through `accim.utils.verification._compute_setpoints_arguments`, and the regimes of `SelectCustomRegime_<zone>` of the custom model), by timestep, with the timesteps per hour of the model, from the first day of its first `RunPeriod` to its last day (`_run_period`), the outdoor metric of the custom model beginning as its programs do; one warning per zone (`_crossing_message`) if the minimum is at or above the cooling setpoint of the thermostats (`ACST`) in some timestep, or the maximum at or below the heating setpoint (`AHST`), with the limit, the extreme setpoint, the number of days and the first day. Left out with a debug message of the logger of the module: the zones of a hook of the API backend, those with the limiter of the drift, those whose arguments `compute_setpoints` rejects, and the models without a `RunPeriod` or a `Timestep` object. `AddAccis` (each output IDF) and `modify_accis` pass their `epw`; the `ValueError` of a minimum not lower than the maximum is kept. Docstrings (module, `AddAccis`, `modify_accis`), section 4.2.3 of the detailed use, `CHANGELOG.md` (the entry of the minimum and maximum), `notes/decisions.md` (G5 points to decision 19), `notes/questions.md` (the details: which setpoints, which days, when `modify_accis` compares, the first timestep of the run period).
- Found while testing it: the first timestep of a run period reads running means of 0 in the EMS (as EnergyPlus does, and `compute_setpoints` reproduces), so with comfort mode 3 of INT EN16798 its setpoints are those below the applicability limits: a minimum of 24 °C crosses the cooling setpoint of 23.9999 °C in that timestep of a run period from 1 June; the warning counts it (documented).
- Files: `accim/sim/ems/setpoint_limits.py`, `accim/sim/accis.py`, `tests/core/test_setpoint_limits.py`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` (§38), `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/questions.md`, `DEVLOG.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
- Verification: `tests/core/test_setpoint_limits.py` 30 passed (4 new instead of the test of the literal warning: no warning without a crossing or without the weather file, and the model unchanged; with a minimum of 24.5 °C and a maximum of 25.5 °C in Seville, INT EN16798, category I, comfort mode 3, one warning per zone with the numbers of a calculation by day with pandas from `compute_setpoints` (65 and 10 days); the minimum of one zone; the run period of the model from June to August; the zones of a hook and of the limiter, and a model without a `RunPeriod`, with their debug messages; `AddAccis` with and without `epw`, with byte-identical output IDFs). `compute_setpoints` takes 0.8 s for a year by timestep. No EMS code changes (golden master unaffected; the warning does not change the IDFs); the changed modules compile with Python 3.9.
- Next step: S2.

  (`AthbSetpointHook.saturated`); `saturated_hours` out of the warm-up days (`WarmupFlag`), `prepare()` (resets them)

### 2026-11-10
#### [done] H2: T0 of `te_ref_nl` from the weather file (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: task H2 of v04 §7 (finding B1-1, verdict #8 in `notes/decisions.md`): van der Linden et al. (2006) define Ti
  with the maximum and minimum of the whole day ("the day under study and the three preceding days", v03 §11), and the
  EMS took for T0 those of the hours of the day so far, so Te,ref drifted within the day.
- Changes: new module `accim.sim.schedule_files` (`write_schedule_file`, `remove_schedule_file`, `schedule_file_path`,
  `model_folder`, `SCHEDULE_TYPE_LIMITS`; shared with H6): a CSV file of hourly values (a header row, one column, the
  values as `repr` writes them) in the folder of the model, and a `Schedule:File` object with its absolute path, Any
  Number, no interpolation, 60 minutes per item and, where the IDD has the field, Adjust Schedule for Daylight Savings
  No. `accim.sim.ems.outdoor_metric`: `MID_RANGE_SCHEDULE` ('ACCIS Outdoor Mid Range'), `MID_RANGE_FILE_SUFFIX`,
  `MID_RANGE_SENSOR` ('OutdoorMidRangeToday'), `mid_range_values(epw)` (the mean of the maximum and minimum of the day
  of each hour, from 1 January, 8760 or 8784 values; `ValueError` for other weather files), `reads_mid_range(idf)`;
  `zone_program_lines(..., mid_range_sensor)`: the branch of `te_ref_nl` sets `OutdoorMidRange` to the sensor or to
  `Tmid_1` (before: the running (max+min)/2 of the day so far, or the outdoor temperature before the end of its first
  hour); `set_outdoor_metric_code` writes the schedule, the CSV file and the sensor when a zone has `te_ref_nl` and the
  weather file is given (removes them otherwise, and with the code), warns without it, and writes the programs again
  (beginning with PMOT) when they begin with the values of a weather file without the sensor and a zone has
  `te_ref_nl`. `get_setpoint_ems_programs`/`print_setpoint_ems_code` (the branch and the legend), `compute_setpoints`
  (it gives the sensor the value of the day in each timestep), and the docstrings of AddAccis, `verify_accim_simulation`
  and `param_accis.modify_CustAST_metric`; section 4.2.2 of the detailed use.
- Measured with EnergyPlus 25.1: the *Schedule Value* sensor of a `Schedule:File` at `BeginTimestepBeforePredictor` has
  the value of the day of the timestep from its first timestep (four days of March, 6 timesteps per hour); EnergyPlus
  read the file with a relative name (next to the IDF, run from another folder) and with an absolute path of 139
  characters (the Input Output Reference recommends a full path and says 100 characters at most).
- Provisional choices (`notes/questions.md`): the CSV file in the folder of the model with its absolute path; the
  schedule only in the models with a zone with `te_ref_nl`, and the programs written again (beginning with PMOT) when
  they begin with a weather file but do not read it.

#### [done] F2: the limiter of the drift of the setpoints by the indoor thermal history (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: task F2 of block F (`notes/PROMPTS_COPILOT_bloque_F_v02.md`, `notes/PROPUESTA_ADAPTACION_INTERIOR_v03.md` §4 and §6), after Parkinson, de Dear & Brager (2020), Energy and Buildings 206, 109559, with decisions 6 and 7 of the author (copied in `notes/decisions.md`).
- Changes:
  - `accim/sim/ems/drift_limiter.py` (new): `DRIFT_MODES`, `ARGUMENTS`, `INPUTS`, `DEFAULTS`, `OUTPUTS`, `check_drift_arguments`, `drift_inputs` (the inputs of a zone from the arguments given, those of the zone or the defaults), `program_lines` (ApplyDriftLimiter_<zone>: the targets AHST − AHSTtol and ACST − ACSTtol; out of the warm-up, the flush of `sum_T_occ_<zone>`/`n_occ_<zone>` at the change of DayOfYear into T_rm_in and the state of the rate, the accumulation of `<zone>_OpT` with `Occ_count_<zone>` > 0, the clamp of the rate or of the window, and AHST/ACST rewritten and rounded where it changes them; `DriftLimitedHours_<zone>`), `init_program_lines`, `set_drift_limiter_code` (inputs declared, programs, the run line before SetASTnoTol, outputs, InitDriftLimiter at BeginNewEnvironment; idempotent), `has_drift_limiter`, `zone_outputs`.
  - `accim/sim/ems/inputs.py`: the six optional inputs, and `input_values` with `drift_inputs`. `accim/sim/generation.py`: the fields of `GenerationArguments` and `zone_input_arguments`. `accim/sim/accis.py`: the arguments of `AddAccis` (validation, `ValueError` with `setpoint_type='pmv'`, `set_drift_limiter_code` for each output IDF at the frequencies of its EMS outputs, `_ems_output_frequencies`) and of `modify_accis` (None keeps them; `drift_inputs` with the inputs of the zone; `ValueError` if no zone has or gets the limiter, or for a model of an earlier version).
  - `accim/utils/ems_code.py`: the arguments in `get_setpoint_ems_code` and `get_setpoint_ems_programs` (InitDriftLimiter first, ApplyDriftLimiter_<zone> before SetASTnoTol, the legend); `accim/sim/preview.py`: `compute_setpoints` raises a `ValueError` with `drift_mode`; `accim/utils/verification.py`: check 4 compares the targets of the zones with the limiter (`Check4_DriftTargetAHST`/`ACST`, with `compute_setpoints` without the minimum and maximum) and not their applied setpoints.
  - Parametric module: `param_accis.modify_DriftMode` (codes 0/1/2 or names; 0 turns it off), `modify_MaxDriftRate`, `modify_AlphaIndoor`, `modify_DeltaCool`, `modify_DeltaHeat`, `modify_InitTrmIn` (`_set_drift_input`); `params_dicts` (predefined and custom models); the classes of `parameters.py`.
  - GUI: `forms.DRIFT_FIELDS`, `drift_arguments` (in `add_accis_arguments` and the calls of the EMS code), `PREDEFINED_MODEL_PARAMETERS`/`CUSTOM_MODEL_PARAMETERS`; `app.py`: the fields in the frame of the minimum and maximum (disabled with the PMV).
  - `Table`: the outputs in `optional_output_columns.ZONE_OUTPUTS`.
  - Docs: section 4.2.7 (new) and 4.7.5 of the detailed use, `parametric_and_optimisation.rst`.
- Measured with EnergyPlus 25.1 (1–30 April, Seville, window in Block1:Zone1 and rate in Block1:Zone2): both backends identical; T_rm_in 25.6–26.3 °C in the first 12 days, the heating setpoint of the window 24.6–25.3 °C instead of 19–20.8 °C (252 occupied hours limited in the month), and the rate never active (the adaptive targets move less than 0.5 K a day).
- Provisional decisions (`notes/questions.md`): the setpoint applied the previous day is that of its last timestep; the days without occupied hours do not change T_rm_in; the parameter `drift_mode` takes the codes 0, 1 and 2, and 0 turns the limiter off.

### 2026-11-10
#### [done] E-F1: the SET of EnergyPlus's Pierce model against pythermalcomfort, measured (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: task E-F1 of the review v04 (§7) and of block F (`notes/PROPUESTA_ADAPTACION_INTERIOR_v03.md` §3 and §5): before F1 (the causal SET of Sun et al. 2026 as a comfort standard, on `SetSetpointHook(method='bisection')`), measure how far the SET of `pythermalcomfort.models.set_tmp` (the one the hooks invert) is from that of EnergyPlus's Pierce two-node model with the same inputs.
- Changes:
  - `accim.sim.people.add_thermal_comfort_model(building, comfort_model, verbose=False)`: adds `'Fanger'`, `'Pierce'` or `'KSU'` (`THERMAL_COMFORT_MODELS`, the choices of the IDDs of EnergyPlus 9.4 to 26.2) in the first empty field Thermal Comfort Model n Type of the People objects (and of the People:Definition objects of the People:Instance objects of EnergyPlus 26.2), keeping the models of the ACCIS; `ValueError` before any change for another model, a People:Instance without its definition or an object without an empty field. `PIERCE_SET_OUTPUT` = `'Zone Thermal Comfort Pierce Model Standard Effective Temperature'` (in the `.rdd` of EnergyPlus 25.1; Engineering Reference 25.2, 19.1.4).
  - Measured (the sample model with the ACCIS, INT EN16798 category II mode 3, `vrf_ac`, a year in Seville, EnergyPlus 25.1, 6 timesteps per hour; `set_tmp` of pythermalcomfort 4.8.1 with the hourly Zone Mean Air Temperature, Zone Mean Radiant Temperature and Zone Air Relative Humidity and the activity, clothing and air velocity schedules of the People objects; body area 1.8258 m², 101325 Pa): pythermalcomfort − EnergyPlus of the hourly means of all the hours of the year, Zone 1 / Zone 2: standing (the posture of EnergyPlus, radiant area 0.72) −0.045 / −0.045 K mean, 0.056 / 0.056 K mean absolute, 0.180 / 0.196 K maximum absolute; sitting (the posture of the hook with 1.2 met or less) −0.116 / −0.116 K, 0.118 / 0.117 K, 0.324 / 0.344 K. The body area and the pressure of the weather file change the result by less than 0.001 K; the difference grows with the SET (about −0.01 K below 26 °C, −0.14 K above 30 °C, standing); the output Zone Thermal Comfort Clothing Value of EnergyPlus 25.1 was 0 with ClothingInsulationSchedule, so the clothing came from the schedule.
  - Recorded in `notes/decisions.md` (the table, the diagnostics and the consequences for F1: the SET of accim is that of EnergyPlus within 0.2 K standing and 0.35 K sitting, slightly lower; F1 keeps the posture of the hook and reports both SET) and in section 4.9.2 of the detailed use.
- Files: `accim/sim/people.py`, `tests/core/test_pierce_set.py`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` (§29), `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.

### 2026-11-10
#### [done] G4: JPN Aqilah 2025 with the weather file, and its static setpoints as arguments (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: task G4 of the review v04 (`notes/PROPUESTAS_MEJORAS_post_1.1.0_v04.md` §3.1, §3.2 and §7, findings B4-1 and B4-2, verdicts #29 and #30, decision §6.2): comfort standard 23 read the RMOT of EnergyPlus, whose first value in a run period is the sum of equation 19.82 not divided by 1 − α⁷, so its comfort temperature was biased low for about three weeks (the YAML twin 101, `rmot_alpha` initialised from the weather file, has no such transient), and its static setpoints were the literals 20/28. Decisions applied: `rmot_alpha` from the weather file when `AddAccis` gets one, RMOT otherwise with a warning; test 23 vs 101; the arguments `static_heating_setpoint`/`static_cooling_setpoint` (20.0/28.0, EMS keys `StaticAHST`/`StaticACST`) in `AddAccis`, `modify_accis` and `zone_arguments`, for the comfort standards with comfort mode 1 whose standard does not fix static setpoints (only 23; the others ignore them), and the parameters of the parametric module.
- Design (the decisions of the task leave it open): the code of 23 reads `Tout_metric` instead of RMOT exactly when the programs of the outdoor metric of the model begin with the values of a weather file (`begins_with_weather_file`), and then its zones get the inputs of `rmot_alpha` (code 2, α 0.8, 30 days, those of the definition with that metric); otherwise its code reads RMOT and the inputs of the outdoor metric of its zones are not used (`uses_outdoor_metric` does not count them). The switch is applied by `set_weather_file_lines` (branch by branch in `SetComfTemp`, `CountHours_<zone>` and `SetVST`, and whole in `SetAST_CS23_<chunk>`), reverted while `set_comfort_standard_code` and `set_outdoor_metric_code` rewrite the chains (whose exact comparisons expect the lines of accim) and applied again at their end; one decision (`outdoor_metric_needed`) for `set_comfort_standard_code`, `modify_accis` and the parametric module. A model whose programs already begin with the values of a weather file keeps them for 23 (`modify_accis` without `epw`, `modify_ComfStand`); if the alpha of a zone that gets 23 changes while the programs are kept, a warning says that its running mean begins with the value of the weather file for its previous alpha. The static setpoints are inputs of the zones of 23 only (`InputVariable.static_only`), so the output IDFs of the other comfort standards do not change; the models with the code of 23 declare them as global variables (`set_static_setpoint_variables`, also in `add_global_variables`, removed by `set_comfort_standard_code` when the code of 23 goes).
- Measurement (`compute_setpoints`, Seville, category 90, comfort mode 3; 23 without the weather file vs 101): on the first day, hours 2 to 24, 23 is 0.92 K (1 January), 1.47 K (1 April) and 1.93 K (1 July) lower; from day 21, hours 2 to 24, at most 0.024, 0.024 and 0.028 K. In the first hour of each day the two differ for one timestep (23 still reads the RMOT of the previous day, 101 has updated its running mean), up to 0.10 K in the hourly mean (0.1013 K on 2 May from 1 January, 0.52 K in that timestep). So the criterion of the task (|ΔComfTemp| < 0.1 K from day 21, > 1 K on day 1) holds for the transient with the starts of 1 April and 1 July and the hours 2 to 24 (and for all the hours of days 21 to 30), not from 1 January (0.92 K on day 1, hours 2 to 24; 1.30 K in the hourly maximum, which includes the first timestep, RMOT 0) nor in every first hour of the year: the test checks it that way and records it.

### 2026-11-10
#### [done] G3: the SET in the callbacks of EnergyPlus (method 'bisection'), its outputs and the documentation of A6 (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: task G3 of the review v04 (`notes/PROPUESTAS_MEJORAS_post_1.1.0_v04.md` §3.1 and §7, findings A6-1 to A6-4, verdict #35): the table of A6 rested on "`set_tmp` cannot run in the callbacks of EnergyPlus", while the review ran it there with Python 3.12 and numba 0.68.1; block F needs the SET in the callbacks; the prepared model had no outputs of the air and radiant temperatures; `position='standing'` by default; the insensitivity of the SET of ASHRAE 55 to low air speeds and the cost of the JIT were undocumented. The rule of the session: if `set_tmp` crashes in the callbacks, warm it up before `run_simulation`, then try `NUMBA_DISABLE_JIT=1`, and record the versions and the outcome here before falling back to the table.
- Experiments (a script outside the repository: the sample model with the ACCIS, 15 July, a hook that calls `set_tmp` with an array of two values and with numbers at every BeginTimestepBeforePredictor, `run_simulation` in its own process; Python 3.14.3, numba 0.68.1, pythermalcomfort 4.8.1, EnergyPlus 25.1, Windows): (1) no call before: the process stops with an access violation (exit code -1073741819), twice, also with the cache of numba on disk; (2) one call with numbers and one with an array before `run_simulation`: completed, 5340 callbacks (first 0.65 ms); (3) one call with numbers only before it: completed (so the specialisation for arrays compiles fine inside the callbacks); (4) `NUMBA_DISABLE_JIT=1`: pythermalcomfort fails (`AttributeError: 'function' object has no attribute 'py_func'`, its `heat_index_lu` uses `.py_func`), so it is not an option; (5) the library of the API of EnergyPlus loaded (`load_energyplus_api('25.1')`) and then the same calls: the process stops at the first call, outside any callback. So the access violation is the first call of `set_tmp` (numba) in a process that has loaded the library of the API of EnergyPlus, not the callback itself; this is why the warm-up of A6 (before the simulation, but in a process with the library loaded, or in the parent of the simulation process) did not help. With an empty cache of numba (`NUMBA_CACHE_DIR`), importing pythermalcomfort took 13.6 s and the first SET 2.3 s; with the cache, 2.0 s and 0.02 s. In `run_ep`, the first version of `prepare_hooks` (after `load_energyplus_api`) broke the process pool (the same access violation in the worker), and moving it before `load_energyplus_api` fixed it.
- Changes:
  - `accim/sim/comfort_hooks.py`: `SetSetpointHook(..., position=None, ..., method='table')`: `method='bisection'` calculates the setpoints in each timestep and the SET of each zone (`SET_<zone>`, from the sensors `SET_MAT_<zone>`, `SET_MRT_<zone>`, `SET_RH_<zone>`), with `prepare()` (one SET with numbers and one with an array of two values); `position=None` is 'sitting' with 1.2 met or less, 'standing' above; `zone_set()`, `keys`. `apply_set_setpoints(..., method='table', output_frequencies=None)`: the three sensors, the outputs of EnergyPlus *Zone Mean Air Temperature*, *Zone Mean Radiant Temperature* and *Zone Air Relative Humidity* of the zones (at the frequencies of the EMS outputs of the model by default, `_model_output_frequencies`), and `SET_<zone>` with the bisection. `set_of_results(results, hook)`: the SET of each zone in each row of the results. `SET_METHODS`, `set_zone_outputs()`. The statement on `set_tmp` reworded in the docstrings (module, class), with the documentation of the JIT cost, of the air speeds below which the SET of ASHRAE 55 does not change (0.1 m/s; about 0.16 m/s at 1.1 met and 0.21 m/s at 1.2 met, from `two_nodes_gagge.py` of pythermalcomfort 4.8.1: `air_speed = max(v, 0.1)` and `h_cc = max(h_cc, 5.66·(met − 0.85)^0.39)`), of the posture and of the weighting of the air and radiant temperatures (measured: posture 0.004–0.008 K heating and 0.05 K cooling; |tr − ta| = 3 K, 0.10 K heating and 0.07 K cooling).
  - `accim/sim/energyplus_api/runner.py`: `prepare_hooks()` (exported by `accim.sim.energyplus_api`), which `run_simulation` calls before `load_energyplus_api`.
  - `accim/data/postprocessing/optional_output_columns.py`: `SET_<zone>` in `Table`, `<zone>_SET (°C)`, averaged, with block and building values (the test of the optional outputs against `accim.sim` checks it).

### 2026-11-10
#### [done] G2: the running mean of ASHRAE 55 of EnergyPlus with a run period from the 2nd to the 30th of January (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: task G2 of the review v04 (`notes/PROPUESTAS_MEJORAS_post_1.1.0_v04.md` §3.1 and §7, finding E1-1, and the doc finding of E2): `energyplus_running_means` and `compute_setpoints` gave a PMOT 0.142361 °C above that of EnergyPlus 25.1 with a run period from 8 January (the P1 task found with B5). The reviewer traced it in `ThermalComfort.cc` (25.1, `CalcThermalComfortAdaptiveASH55`): for a run period that begins on day D of January with 2 ≤ D ≤ 30, the branch that wraps the year reads the days 1 to D − 1 of January and then the last days of December, so the scalar `avgDryBulbASH` keeps the mean of 31 December, and the first `BeginDayFlag` writes it over the mean of D − 1 January (the slot 30 of the window), where it stays for 30 days; on 1 January and from the 31st the last day read is the day before the run period, so nothing changes; RMOT is not affected.
- Changes:
  - `accim/sim/preview.py`: in `_energyplus_daily`, the daily mean of the day before the run period is that of 31 December in the windows of PMOT when the run period begins on the 2nd to the 30th of January; docstrings of `energyplus_running_means` and `compute_setpoints`.

  - Docs: sections 4.3.1 and 4.6.1 of the detailed use. `CHANGELOG.md`: the entries of E1 (also "a year in a few seconds" instead of "about a second", v04 §3.5: 3.3 s measured by the reviewer) and E2. `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` §24, `TODO.md` (the P1 task of the 0.14 °C closed), `notes/work-log.md`.

The counters run inside `RunACCIS_<zone>`, with the variables of each zone, right after SetAST and SetASTnoTol, while the hooks of the controller run after all the programs of the calling point, so the setpoints of the hook have to enter the programs of the zone right after SetAST: option (a) is viable that way.

- Context: A6 is the last proposal of the list of the author. v03 (A6, no prompt, and no adjustment in the brief) gives two ways for the setpoints of the SET: (a) a proportional control in Erl of the temperature setpoint from the SET of the previous timestep (`T_sp = T_sp_prev + k·(SET_target − SET_prev)`, with saturation, k ≈ 0.8; "sencillo pero con retardo y posible oscilación") and (b) a hook of the API backend, as A4, with `pythermalcomfort.models.set_tmp` and bisection on the operative temperature ("exacto"); the setpoints of the PPD of the categories of ISO 7730:2025 (Table A.1: I < 6 %, II < 10 %, III < 15 %, IV < 25 %), equivalent to the PMV ±0.2/0.5/0.7/1.0, "se resuelve con A2"; and names the setpoints by cooling effect (the outputs of the model `CoolingEffectASH55` of the People objects) without a design. It gives no defaults for the SET setpoints, the metabolic rate, the clothing or the air speed. pythermalcomfort 4.8.1 was installed for A4.

  - `accim/sim/comfort_hooks.py` (new): `athb_met`, `athb_clo`, `athb_pmv` (an `ImportError` that says `pip install accim[comfort]`), `ATHB_TEMPERATURE_RANGE` (10–30 °C, the air temperatures with which ISO 7730:2025 uses the PMV, v03 C1), `AthbSetpointHook` (checked arguments; `operative_temperatures(humidity, trm)`: both setpoints by one vectorised bisection, the limits of the range outside it; `__call__` at `BEGINTIMESTEPBEFOREPREDICTOR`: `RMOT`, the sensors of each zone, `AHST_Act_<zone>`/`ACST_Act_<zone>`, `ATHB_PMV/clo/met_<zone>`; `setpoints`; picklable), `apply_athb_setpoints` (the ACCIS of accim 1.0.0 or later, full air-conditioning, once: sensors `ATHB_MAT/MRT/RH_<zone>` with the key of `<zone>_OpT`, global variables, averaged EMS outputs, `Output:Variable` objects), `zone_outputs`.
  - `accim/run/run.py`: `run_ep(..., hooks=None)`: with `backend='api'`, the `after_programs` of `run_simulations`; with `'ems'`, `NotImplementedError`.
  - `setup.py`: the extra `comfort` (`pythermalcomfort>=4.8; python_version >= "3.11"`).

  - Deviations and decisions taken provisionally (in `notes/questions.md`): full air-conditioning only (in mixed mode the windows follow the setpoints of the ACCIS); the setpoints in every timestep, as the ACCIS; the range of 10–30 °C (an argument); the ATHB_PMV of each zone from its air and radiant temperatures; at the first timestep of each environment the running mean is 0 (as for the ACCIS); no cache (about 1 ms for each zone and timestep: a week of two zones in 16 s). Test (3) of v03 (ATHB_PMV within the setpoints in the hours with the HVAC on) does not hold strictly in the sample model, whose VRF exceeds the cooling setpoint by up to 1.0 K while it cools (690 timesteps of 1008; ATHB_PMV up to 0.63), and the ATHB_PMV of the zone uses its air and radiant temperatures; the tests check instead that the setpoints of the schedules are those of the bisection. The sensors read the last values of the previous timestep, and the outputs are the means of each timestep, so the ATHB_PMV of the outputs is not that of the reported temperatures (58 of 1007 timesteps equal within 1e-9); the setpoints are (exact with the humidity and the running mean of the previous row). The arguments of the hook are not in the model, so `verify_accim_simulation` does not check these setpoints (`TODO.md`).

- Context: C2 is the next proposal of the list of the author. v03 asks, reusing D1, for `hours_outside_range` (Method A), `degree_hours` (Method B) and `ppd_weighted_hours` (Method C) of CEN/TR 16798-2:2019 (Annex D) in `objectives.py`, by zone and building and by warm and cold period, with the categories of EN 16798-1 as ranges, and the acceptable deviations of Table E.1 (Annex E) as constraints of the optimisation; it has no prompt, and the brief no adjustment; CIBSE TM52/TM59 are out of scope (D12).
- Changes:
  - The reducers of besos get one output each (`EPResults(name, frequency, units, data)`, read in `besos/objectives.py`), while the indicators need the operative temperature, the limits and the occupants together, so the EMS counts them. `accim/sim/ems/long_term_discomfort.py` (new): with `report_long_term_discomfort=True`, each zone gets the program `CountLongTermDiscomfort_<zone>`, which `RunACCIS_<zone>` runs right after `CountHoursNoApp_<zone>`: in each occupied timestep, `Hours Above Upper Limit_<zone>` and `Hours Below Lower Limit_<zone>` are the length of the timestep if the operative temperature is above `ACSTnoTol` or below `AHSTnoTol` (Method A), and `Degree Hours Above Upper Limit_<zone>` and `Degree Hours Below Lower Limit_<zone>` the difference times that length (Method B, units `Kh`); 0 without occupants; summed outputs. The building gets their sums (`..._Building`) from the program `CountLongTermDiscomfort_Building` (one zone per line, so the lines do not grow with the zones; eppy writes programs of more than the 131 lines of the IDD), whose calling manager is after those of the zones (the order of the IDF, as `programs.py` already assumes). `set_long_term_discomfort_code(idf, frequencies)` adds them to a model of `add_accis_to_idf`; `has_long_term_discomfort`, `zone_lines`, `building_lines`, `output_names`, `OUTPUTS`.
  - `accim/sim/accis.py`: `report_long_term_discomfort` (False by default) of `AddAccis` and `add_accis_to_idf` (`ValueError` if it is not a boolean, or with `setpoint_type='pmv'`: the inputs of the zones keep their defaults there, so the setpoints of the ACCIS are not those of the control); with `output_type='simplified'`, their `Output:Variable` objects (`_request_outputs`, which `_add_hours_below_outputs` now uses too); `AddAccis.arguments` records it.
  - `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/objectives.py`: `hours_outside_range` and `degree_hours` (checked sums that check the name of the output, `'EMS,<output>_<zone>'`, when the result has it) and `deviation_constraint_bound(occupied_hours, period, level)` (`'<=126.0'` for 2100 h, yearly, 6 %, the format of the constraint bounds of besos and platypus); `accim/data/postprocessing/long_term_discomfort.py`: `deviation_limit` (Table E.1), which `deviation_check` now uses (same expression). `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`: the four classes forward `report_long_term_discomfort` to `add_accis_to_idf`.

- Context: E2 is the next proposal of the list of the author (brief `notes/COPILOT_BRIEF_v1.2.0_v01.md`). v03 asks `verify_accim_simulation` to check ePMV = e·PMV, aPMV = PMV/(1 + λ·PMV), the corrected activity level, `Tout_metric` against `outdoor_metrics()` (B1), the floor and ceiling of B5 and, for B3, the setpoints of the registered models against `compute_setpoints`; the brief adds ePMV only with PMV > 0 and the metabolic correction "with the tolerance of 5.5–6.1 %". ### 2026-11-09
#### [done] E1: the setpoints of the ACCIS without EnergyPlus, `accim.sim.preview` (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: E1 is the first proposal of the list of the author for this session (brief `notes/COPILOT_BRIEF_v1.2.0_v01.md`, decisions D1-D12; D2 was done first because it was in progress). The proposal asks for `outdoor_metrics`, `compute_setpoints` (the Erl of the setpoints run with the Erl interpreter of accim, with the running means as EnergyPlus calculates them, Engineering Reference 25.2, 19.1) and `plot_setpoints`, with tests against the hourly CSV of EnergyPlus for comfort standards 1, 2 and 99 within the rounding of `SetpointAcc`; the brief adds the outdoor series with which B1 will initialise its running means (D3) and D9 (reproduce the running means of EnergyPlus). `get_setpoint_ems_code` cuts the subprograms to the lines of the comfort mode, so its text cannot be run. Five simulations of the sample model with EnergyPlus 25.1 (outside the repository: comfort standard 1, a year, hourly; 2, 10/03 to 20/04, hourly; 1, 0 and 99, some days in timesteps) and its source code (`ThermalComfort.cc` of 25.1) showed how EnergyPlus calculates the running means: ASHRAE 55, the simple mean of the 30 previous daily means, those of the weather file before the run period; CEN 15251, equation 19.82 on the first day without dividing by 1 − α⁷ (5.92 °C on 1 January in Seville, against 8.34 °C of EN 16798-1 B.1), then 19.83; the daily mean of a simulated day, that of the first timestep of each hour, interpolated; and the EMS sensors read the running means of the previous day at the first timestep of each day, 0 at the first timestep of the run period.
- Changes:
  - New module `accim/sim/preview.py`: `read_epw_dry_bulb`; `outdoor_metrics(epw, alpha=0.8, days=30, frequency='hourly')` (daily mean, maximum and minimum, EN 16798-1 B.1 with any α for a year that repeats, B.2, the mean of the n previous days, the Te,ref of van der Linden et al. 2006, the monthly mean); `energyplus_running_means(epw, timesteps_per_hour=6, start_date=1)` (the running means of EnergyPlus for each day of a run period); `compute_setpoints(epw, comfort_standard, category=None, comfort_mode=None, *, timesteps_per_hour=6, start_date=1, frequency='hourly', **custom_arguments)` (the programs run in each timestep in the order of `RunACCIS_<zone>`, from the state of `InitASTvariables`, with `CountHours_<zone>` for the applicability; the hourly values are the means of the timesteps); `plot_setpoints`.
  - `accim/utils/ems_code.py`: `get_setpoint_ems_programs` (the programs by name, with the whole subprograms; the category and the comfort mode are required when the comfort standard has them) and `_checked_arguments`, shared with `get_setpoint_ems_code`, whose text does not change.
  - Docs: section 4.3.1 of the detailed use (new); the API page of the module; the docstrings of `accim.sim` and `accim.utils`. `CHANGELOG.md` (Added), `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` §8, `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/questions.md` (the first days of `outdoor_metrics`, decided provisionally), `TODO.md` (A5 unblocked; the `.stat` file and leap years; two possible errors of comfort standards 9 and 10 for the author: their running means look swapped, and the heating setpoint of comfort mode 1 of 9 out of the limits has the sign of the cooling one).

The GUI, `print_setpoint_ems_code` and `verify_accim_simulation` do not change: E1 adds no arguments or outputs to the models (E2 will use `compute_setpoints`).

#### [done] D2: `Table` with the simulations of the setpoints based on the PMV (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: The author included D2 in 1.2.0 ("Columnas de los índices nuevos en `Table`": recognise `ePMV_<zone>`, `e_<zone>`, `aPMV_<zone>` and, in v03 of the proposals, `Activity_corrected_<zone>`, in the renaming of `rename_cols`, in `time_plot` and in `wrangled_table`), and later his session plan (E1 → D2 → E2...). D2 was in progress when the plan arrived, so it was finished first. A simulation of 3 days of July of the sample model with ePMV setpoints and the metabolic correction (EnergyPlus 25.1, a script outside the repository) showed that `Table` could not read their CSV files at all (`ValueError: Columns must be same length as key`: `run_ep` names them `<IDF>[<EPW>.csv`, and `Table` split every name into the 12 parts of the names of the output IDFs of `AddAccis`, also from the GUI); with a name of 12 parts, it added up the index, the expectancy factor (16.8 instead of 0.7 in a day), the setpoints, the schedules and the mean air temperature with `frequency_agg_func='sum'`, converted the corrected activity level (W/person) into kWh/m2, kept the EMS names, dropped them with `standard_outputs=True`, and gave infinite block and building totals of energy per m2 (no floor areas in these simulations).
- Changes:
  - `accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`: `_name_fields` (names with 2 parts: `Model` and `EPW`, and `CS_X`... `NS_X`; other names, a clear `ValueError`); `_averaged_outputs` (those of the setpoints, but the hour counters); the outputs of the setpoints kept with `standard_outputs=True`; the outputs of the metabolic correction of a People object of one zone get the suffix of the zone in `_occupied_zones` (the mean in a zone with several People objects); the corrected activity level is not converted into energy; `Zone Mean Air Temperature` renamed and with levels, as the operative temperature; `_add_pmv_index_levels` (block and building values, at once); the renaming of `rename_cols`; `format_table('comfort hours')` with the hour counters of the setpoints; `per_area` (the block and building totals per m2 are NaN, listed, where the floor area is 0); `_zorder` (the figures draw the series with `Setpoint` over the others).
  - New module `accim/data/postprocessing/pmv_index_columns.py`: the columns of `Table` of these outputs, from the names of `accim.sim.pmv_index`, which has `zone_outputs()`, `PMV_SETPOINT_SCHEDULES` and `METABOLIC_CORRECTION_OUTPUTS` (`_add_outputs` and the metabolic correction write their outputs from them; the golden master does not change).
  - Names in `Table`: `<zone>_aPMV`, `<zone>_ePMV`, `<zone>_Adaptive Coefficient`, `<zone>_Expectancy Factor`, `<zone>_Heating Setpoint (aPMV)`, `<zone>_Cooling Setpoint_No Tolerance (aPMV)`, `<zone>_Comfortable Hours (h)`..., `<zone>_Thermostat Heating Setpoint (PMV)`, `<zone>_Zone Mean Air Temperature (°C)`, `<zone>_Metabolic Correction Factor`, `<zone>_Corrected Activity Level (W/person)`; no name contains another one, which the figures and `wrangled_table` would also take. `PMV_<zone>` of the PMV setpoints, `Occupied hours_<zone>` and the metabolic correction of a People object of several zones keep their EMS names (decision record).
  - Docs: section 4.7.3 of the detailed use (new) and a pointer from 4.8; the docstrings of `Table` and `format_table`; the API page of the module. `CHANGELOG.md` (Added; Fixed: the aggregation and the energy per m2), `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` §7, `notes/decisions.md`, `TODO.md` (the columns of B1, B5, D1 and A4, blocked).

#### [done] `accim.sim.people.set_clothing_method` (A5), with the `schedule` that the author chose; the dynamic clothing model checked in Schiavon and Lee (2013) and ASHRAE 55-2020 (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: The author answered the open question of `notes/questions.md` with "Opción (b)": the `schedule` of the proposed `set_clothing_method(building, method, schedule=None)` is the clothing insulation schedule of `'ClothingInsulationSchedule'`, and `'CalculationMethodSchedule'` needs another argument. Then he gave the folders of the articles that §9.2 of the proposals lacked: `D:\DSG\Otros articulos pendientes` (Schiavon and Lee 2013, Aqilah et al. 2025 and Schweiker 2022) and the library `D:\DSG\Mendeley library` (where ANSI/ASHRAE 55-2020 is "Arens et al._2021_Thermal Environmental Conditions for Human Occupancy").
- Changes:
  - `accim/sim/people.py`: `set_clothing_method(building, method, schedule=None, calculation_method_schedule=None, verbose=True)` sets the *Clothing Insulation Calculation Method* of all the People objects (and People:Instance objects): `'ClothingInsulationSchedule'`, `'DynamicClothingModelASHRAE55'` or `'CalculationMethodSchedule'` (`CLOTHING_METHODS`, in any case). `schedule` is the clothing insulation schedule (with the first method, and for the periods of the method 1 of the third one; an error with the dynamic model; without it, the People objects keep theirs), and `calculation_method_schedule`, named as its field in the IDD, the schedule that chooses the method, required with `'CalculationMethodSchedule'` and an error with the others. Before changing anything, it checks the method, that the schedules are in the model and that the values of the calculation method schedule are 1 and 2 (`schedule_values` of `accim.sim.hvac.schedules`); it warns about the People objects without a clothing insulation schedule with the methods that read it.
  - Docs: section 4.8 of the detailed use (`set_clothing_method`, with an example; the sources of the equations of the dynamic model and the note of ASHRAE 55-2020); `CHANGELOG.md`; `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` (§1, §4, §5, §6); `notes/decisions.md` (the decision); `notes/questions.md` (answered); `TODO.md` (only the part of A5 that needs E1 is left); a note in §9.2 of the proposals (v02): the items 1, 2 and 7 are available, and 4, 5 and 8 are not in the library.
- Findings:
  - With `'CalculationMethodSchedule'`, EnergyPlus 25.1 reports in `Zone Thermal Comfort Clothing Value` the clothing insulation of the schedule in the periods of the method 1 (1 clo), unlike `'ClothingInsulationSchedule'` (0), and that of the dynamic model in those of the method 2; no severe errors.
  - The equations of the dynamic model in the proposal, the documentation and the tests are equation 10 of Schiavon and Lee (2013, §3.6) and those of Figure 5-1 of ANSI/ASHRAE 55-2020 (5.2.2.2 g, and "Method 4" of its informative appendix), which accepts the model in mechanically conditioned buildings and notes that it is based on field studies and may not be appropriate for all cultures and occupancy types; the standard does not apply to occupants above 1.5 clo (5.2.2.3).
- Files: `accim/sim/people.py`, `tests/core/test_pmv_input_parameters.py`, `tests/core/test_people_instance.py`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md`, `TODO.md`, `DEVLOG.md`, `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/questions.md`, `notes/work-log.md`, `notes/PROPUESTAS_MEJORAS_post_1.1.0_v02.md`.

- Context: The second part of the request of the author ("cuando termines, vamos a continuar con las tareas de los cambios de la v1.2.0 ... todo lo que no puedas hacer, añádelo a las tareas pendientes del TODO.md, pero no quiero que te inventes nada"). A5 was the P1 proposal of block A left pending (`TODO.md`); its prompt asks for `clothing_insulation` as a number, `'dynamic_ashrae55'` or a dictionary by season aligned with the cooling season, the same dictionary for `activity_level`, and tests of the fields of the People objects and of the schedules on the days 120, 121, 274 and 275. Then the author gave the standards that §9.2 of the proposals (v02) lacked: UNE-EN 16798-1:2020 (EN 16798-1:2019), UNE-CEN/TR 16798-2:2019 and UNE-EN ISO 7730:2025, in `D:\DSG\Normativas` (on the server).
- Changes:
  - `accim/sim/apmv.py`, `set_pmv_input_parameters()`: `activity_level` and `clothing_insulation` take a dictionary by season (`{'heating_season': x, 'cooling_season': y}`), for the model or as the value of a zone in the dictionaries by zone, which gives a `Schedule:Compact` with the value of the cooling season from `cooling_season_start` to the day before `cooling_season_end` (new keyword-only arguments after `verbose`, 121 and 274 by default, as the cooling season of the setpoints; `_seasonal_schedule_fields`, `_day_of_year`); `clothing_insulation='dynamic_ashrae55'` sets the method `DynamicClothingModelASHRAE55` of the field *Clothing Insulation Calculation Method* of the People objects (of the People:Instance objects in EnergyPlus 26.2), and a number or a dictionary by season sets `ClothingInsulationSchedule` back if the field had another method. Each call writes the schedules of the parameters that it receives again (`_write_compact_schedule`): before, a new call kept the schedule of the first one with its value (reproduced: 0.5 and then 1.0 left 0.5).
  - The standards: the categories of the PMV cited by section 4.8, the docstring of `apply_pmv_setpoints` and `PMV_INDICES['pmv']` are the categories I to III of Table A.1 of ISO 7730:2025 and of Table B.1 of EN 16798-1 (both also have IV, ±1). ISO 7730:2025 names I to III the categories A to C of 2005, so the ePMV notebook says "the category II of EN 16798-1 and ISO 7730" instead of "the category B of ISO 7730" (run and exported again: 45 s, the same results). The clothing insulation of the example of A5 (1 and 0.5 clo) is the one that EN 16798-1 (Table B.2) and ISO 7730:2025 (A.4) assume in the heating and cooling seasons. The PMV program of Annex D of ISO 7730:2025, written in Python outside the repository, reproduces its Table D.1 within 0.01 (runs 3, 7 and 10, rounding), and with it the approximation of the metabolic correction of section 4.8.1: with the air temperature equal to the mean radiant temperature, 0.1 m/s and 50 % relative humidity, 6.1 % (PMV 0.914 against 0.905 for Fanger and Toftum) with 1.2 met and 5.5 % (0.823 against 0.782) with 1 met; the same reductions from 0.1 to 0.3 m/s and from 40 to 70 %, and PMV within ±0.01. In CEN/TR 16798-2:2019, the long-term evaluation of the comfort (proposals C2 and D1) is in clause 8 and Annex D, not in an Annex F as the proposals say.
  - Docs: section 4.8 of the detailed use (the values by season and the dynamic model, with an example) and 4.8.1 (the conditions of the approximation); `CHANGELOG.md` (Added, and Fixed for the reuse of the schedules); `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` (§1, §3, the new §4 of A5 and §6 of the standards, §5 of the tests; the proposals are in git since `e238dad`); `TODO.md` (A5 done; its two parts not done and the tests with EnergyPlus 26.x, pending); `notes/decisions.md`; a dated note in §9.2 of `notes/PROPUESTAS_MEJORAS_post_1.1.0_v02.md`.
- Findings (EnergyPlus 25.1, the sample model of two zones in Seville):
  - EnergyPlus applies the schedules in the local time of the daylight saving time of the model: in the standard time of the results, the schedule by season changes at 23:00 of the day before, and the EMS program of the cooling season (`DayOfYear`) at 0:00 of the same day.
  - The EPW of Seville has no leap years, so a run period of 2004 has no 29 February: the EMS changes the season on 1 May, as the schedule.
  - With `DynamicClothingModelASHRAE55`, EnergyPlus takes the outdoor air temperature of the first timestep after 6 a.m. and applies the equations of ASHRAE 55 (Schiavon and Lee, 2013) of the proposal, and keeps the value until then on the next day (0.75 clo before the first one of each run period); checked in three of the four ranges (Seville was never below −5 °C). The output `Zone Thermal Comfort Clothing Value` reports the clothing insulation of the dynamic model only (0 with the schedule).
  - The field and its option are in the People objects of the IDDs of 9.4, 9.6, 25.1 and 25.2, and in the People:Instance objects of the IDD of the tag `v26.2.0` of EnergyPlus.

### 2026-11-08
#### [done] One engine for the setpoints based on indices derived from the PMV (A2), and the PMV setpoints (branch `v1.2.0`)
- Context: The author asked for the new functions of his file of proposals after 1.1.0 (`notes/PROPUESTAS_MEJORAS_post_1.1.0_v01.md`, block A: comfort indices derived from the PMV) in a new branch `v1.2.0`; he chose its P1 proposals, in the order A2, A1, A5, with one commit for each, pushed to GitHub. A2 first: one engine, so that the ePMV of A1 does not copy the code of the aPMV.
- Changes:
  - `accim/sim/pmv_index.py` (new): `apply_pmv_index_setpoints(building, index, index_parameters, ...)` and the registry `PMV_INDICES` of `PmvIndex` objects (`'apmv'` and `'pmv'`) with what depends on the index: its name in the EMS objects, its parameters by zone, the coefficient of each season, the inverse (the PMV setpoint of the thermostat) and the monitor (the index of the zone). The infrastructure, sensors, actuators, global variables, programs, calling managers and outputs of `apmv.py` moved there with the same code, except the names of the functions and the lines that depend on the index; `arguments_table` is the generic `generate_df_from_args`. A model has the setpoints of one index only (`ValueError`).
  - `accim/sim/apmv.py`: `apply_apmv_setpoints` calls the engine (the same signature, defaults and names of accim 0.x); new `apply_pmv_setpoints` (the index `'pmv'`: no coefficient and no monitor, since the index of the zone is its PMV sensor); `generate_df_from_args` calls `arguments_table`.
  - `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/funcs_for_besos/param_apmv.py`: `_set_input_line` finds the lines of `set_zone_input_data_<zone>` by the names of their variables (before, the modifiers wrote the lines 1 to 4, which are other lines in the models of other indices); `_get_apmv_program_targets`, no longer used, removed.
  - Docs: sections 4.8 (the PMV setpoints, the engine, the index of each zone calculated from the PMV of the previous timestep) and 4.9 (the backends with the thermal comfort thermostats) of the detailed use, `docs/source/api/accim.sim.rst` (`accim.sim.pmv_index`), the docstring of `accim.sim`; `CHANGELOG.md`; `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md` (new); `notes/decisions.md`.
- Findings:
  - The aPMV of each zone and the hours within the setpoints are calculated at `BeginTimestepBeforePredictor`, from the PMV of the previous timestep: in a simulation, the PMV that the EMS reports in each timestep is the PMV of EnergyPlus of the previous one.
  - With the thermal comfort thermostats, the EMS and API backends give the same setpoints and EMS variables, but the results of EnergyPlus differ by rounding errors: 2.9e-10 °C in the zone temperatures and 7e-4 J in the meters, in three days of July of the sample model with two zones and VRF systems. It already happened with accim 1.1.0 and the aPMV (a worktree of `master` gives the same maximum difference as the branch); with the models of `AddAccis`, the CSV files are identical. Its cause, inside EnergyPlus, was not looked for.
- Files: `accim/sim/pmv_index.py`, `accim/sim/apmv.py`, `accim/sim/__init__.py`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/funcs_for_besos/param_apmv.py`, `tests/core/test_pmv_index.py`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `docs/source/api/accim.sim.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.2.0.md`, `DEVLOG.md`, `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.

#### [done] The size of the results of the simulations, estimated before running them (`accim.run.estimate_results_size`, and in `AddAccis`) (branch `v1.1.0`)
- Context: The author asked: "la próxima tarea es añadir función o método para estimar el peso de los resultados de simulación, de forma similar al existente en parametric_and_optimisation, pero que funcione con el módulo run; me gustaría que se pudiera estimar sin simulación, o de forma más exacta, con simulación, y que el usuario lo pueda elegir", and then "creo que también estaría bien conocer el peso estimado cuando se genera el idf, para poder decidir sobre los outputs". The estimate of the parametric module is the memory of the hourly tables (`get_hourly_df_parametric`); for `run_ep`, the weight of the results is that of its files on disk (and the memory of the CSV values is also given).
- Measurements (scripts in the session folder, not in the repository): the files of 9 simulations of calibration with EnergyPlus 25.1 (the sample model with the outputs of `AddAccis` at every frequency, 5ZoneAirCooled with its own outputs and with those of `ex_ac`, with design days and with SQLite): the ESO and CSV files are 90 to 95 % of the results; an ESO line of about 16.6 bytes for each hourly or timestep value, 52 to 68 bytes for each daily, monthly or run period value (with the minimum and the maximum), 39 bytes for each time stamp; the CSV has a row for each time stamp of the most frequent frequency, with a comma for each column and about 10.5 bytes for each value; the ESO has the design days when they are simulated, and the CSV too, unless `OutputControl:Files` has `Output CSV` (as in the output IDFs of `AddAccis`): then EnergyPlus writes it with the run periods only; `Output:Meter` goes to the ESO and the MTR files, `MeterFileOnly` to the MTR file only; the SQL file has about 20 bytes for each value and, with `SimpleAndTabular`, the tables. A standard hourly output IDF of the sample model writes 30.1 MB in each simulation (ESO 16.3 MB, CSV 11.3 MB, MTR and Meter.csv 2.0 MB, other files 0.5 MB), so 140 output IDFs with 6 EPWs take about 25 GB.
- Changes:
  - `accim/sim/results_size.py` (new): `estimate_simulation_size(idf)` estimates, without simulation, the size of the files of a simulation (`SimulationSize`: the bytes of the ESO, CSV, MTR, Meter.csv, SQL and other files, the cells of the CSV file, the series at each frequency and notes): the series of the `Output:Variable` objects (each key of each variable; with the key `*`, the objects that report it, `_keys`: zones, People objects, coils, VRF units, AirflowNetwork zones and surfaces with openings, plant loops, nodes...; the EMS output variables, one; the others, one each, named in the notes) and of the `Output:Meter` objects, times the times each one is reported in the run periods and in the simulated sizing periods (`SimulationControl`), times the bytes measured. Three cases found in the calibration: accim requests the HVAC demand of the facility with its name before and after EnergyPlus 9.4, and only one exists; the `District...` meters do not exist without district heating or cooling or ideal loads objects; the operative temperature of the thermostats is reported for the zones with a thermostat only. Notes for the variables reported at each HVAC timestep (`Detailed`), the `HVACTemplate` objects (not expanded) and the EDD file of `debug_ems=True`, not estimated (it can take gigabytes).
  - `accim/run/results_size.py` (new), exported by `accim.run`: `estimate_results_size(simulate=False, only_accim_idfs=None, idfs=None, epws=None, processes=2, verbose=True)`: for the IDFs and EPWs of the working folder (or of `idfs` and `epws`), groups the IDFs with the same output objects and number of objects of each type, read as text (`_outputs_signature`; eppy takes about 0.7 s for each output IDF), reads one IDF of each group with eppy and estimates it or, with `simulate=True`, runs it with the first EPW as `run_ep` runs it (EnergyPlus `-r -x -s C -p 'IDF[EPW'`), in a temporary folder that is also its working folder, and measures its files (if the simulation fails, a warning and the estimate). It prints the size of each simulation and of all of them, after `remove_files()` and the memory of the CSV values, and returns a table with a row for each IDF. `run_ep` points to it.
  - `AddAccis` estimates the size of the results of each model after adding its outputs, prints it before asking whether to write the output IDFs (with the size of all the output IDFs with one EPW), so that other outputs can be chosen, and keeps it in `results_size`; an estimate that fails only warns.
  - Documentation: `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md` (4.1, the transcript of 4.4 and 4.6), `docs/source/9_migration_guide.md` (9.4), the API reference (`sphinx-apidoc`: `accim.run.results_size`, `accim.sim.results_size`), `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.1.0.md` (new section 9; the tests, section 10), `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
- Files: `accim/sim/results_size.py` (new), `accim/run/results_size.py` (new), `accim/run/__init__.py`, `accim/run/run.py`, `accim/sim/accis.py`; `tests/core/test_results_size.py` (new); the documentation above.
- Verification:
  - Calibration: the totals of the 9 simulations within ±8 % (the standard hourly output IDF of the sample model, 0.4 %); validation with 7 example files of EnergyPlus that were not used to calibrate: within ±10 % for the results of more than 5 MB, and within ±45 % for those of 1 MB or less, where the other files (estimated more roughly) are a larger part.
  - `estimate_results_size` on a folder with 4 output IDFs (two groups) and 2 EPWs: 2.2 s without simulation (206.1 MB), 66 s with simulation (206.3 MB, the sizes of the earlier simulations), and no files left in the folder or in the temporary folder.
  - `tests/core/test_results_size.py` (10 tests: the series of a small model, the design days, the leap year, the SQL file and the notes, the groups, the errors, the estimate when the simulation fails, a simulation of January of an output IDF of the sample model, whose estimate is within 15 % of its ESO and CSV files, and the message of `AddAccis` before the question); the test of the simulation fails with other bytes for the hourly values (checked with a pytest plugin).
  - `python -m pytest tests/core tests/parametric_and_optimisation -q -p no:cacheprovider`: 1362 passed (10 more: `test_results_size.py`). The documentation builds with the same warnings as before.
- Next step: committed and pushed to `v1.1.0` at the request of the author; his review.

#### [done] The EMS code of the setpoints before adding the ACCIS (`print_setpoint_ems_code`), the How-to pages generated again, and the indentation of an output IDF (branch `v1.1.0`)
- Context: The author asked: "creo que estaría bien que el usuario pudiera conocer el EMS program de las consignas que se va a usar antes de implementarlo con accim, principalmente para conocer la lógica de qué consignas se van a usar y qué sucede cuando se exceden los límites de aplicabilidad; por ejemplo, una función de ayuda que te permitiera visualizarlo"; then, to do the pending task of `TODO.md` (the How-to pages that show EMS programs); and "quiero que me enseñes un ejemplo de la indentación del código EMS en el idf; el código debería estar indentado al generar el idf, sin modificar nada más".
- Changes:
  - `accim/utils/ems_code.py` (new), exported by `accim.utils`: `get_setpoint_ems_code(comfort_standard, category, comfort_mode, ...)` gives as text the EMS code with which the ACCIS calculates the setpoints, and `print_setpoint_ems_code(...)` prints it; they take the arguments of `modify_accis`, with one value each and the same defaults. The code is built with the functions that write the output IDFs, indented, in the order in which `RunACCIS_<zone>` runs it: the inputs of the setpoints (`SetInputData_<zone>`, with those of the custom model for `ComfStand` 99), `SetAppLimits`, `ApplyCAT` and `SetComfTemp` (the branch of the comfort standard, or `return`), `SetAST`, `SetAST_Master`, the lines of the comfort mode in its subprogram `SetAST_CS<n>_<chunk>` with their rounding (`setast.subprogram_name`, new; without a comfort mode, the subprograms whole) and `SetASTnoTol`, after comments that name the comfort standard, the category and the comfort mode and explain the variables. A comfort standard that does not exist, a category that is not one of the comfort standard and a comfort mode without setpoints raise a `ValueError`, as in `AddAccis`.
  - How-to pages `full_example_IBPSA` and `using_apmv_setpoints` generated again (`docs/prepare_howto_notebooks.py run` and `export`, `docs/README.md` section 7.2; 378 and 38 s, no problems), and their item removed from `TODO.md`. Compared with the pages of 1.0.0 (section 7.4): an output IDF has 34 EMS programs instead of 78, the program `apply_aPMV_<zone>` is indented, the model is an IDF of eppy instead of besos, and logs, line numbers of warnings and times change. The results of the full example also change slightly (the tables of cells 92 to 103 and three figures; for instance, the heating demand of `BRA_Stat_MM`, 130.42 to 130.20 kWh/m2): the notebook run with the code of `a235b1c9` (before the design days of `vrf_mm`, through `PYTHONPATH`, section 7.5) gives the results of the pages of 1.0.0 exactly, so they only come from the design days of `vrf_mm` (also those of `hvac_mode` 1, whose VRF systems are sized again).
  - The indentation of an output IDF, shown to the author (nothing changed): an output IDF of `AddAccis` (the sample model, `vrf_mm`, INT EN16798, category II, comfort mode 3) has its 34 EMS programs indented, each one as `indent_lines` writes its lines; the same IDF generated with the indentation off (`indent_lines` replaced in that process only) has the same 6962 lines, and the 1071 that differ only differ in spaces (the indentation, and the alignment of the comments `!-`).
  - Documentation: `CHANGELOG.md` (Added: the functions; Changed: the pages), `docs/source/4_detailed use.md` (4.3, with an example), `docs/source/api/accim.utils.rst` (`sphinx-apidoc`), `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.1.0.md` (sections 7 and 9), `TODO.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
