#### [done] `ex_ac` and `ex_mm` with the example files of EnergyPlus: fixes for any HVAC system and the windows of mixed mode (branch `v1.1.0`)
- Context: The author asked: "quiero que trabajemos en el script type ex_mm y ex_ac; quiero que pruebes distintos idfs que te inventes o existentes modificados con distintos HVAC system para comprobar que funciona en cualquiera, independientemente de todo (siempre y cuando el modelo original funcione bien, claro)", then "ten en cuenta que en el ex_mm puede haber ventilación scheduled o con airflow network" and "las ventanas se deben poder abrir y cerrar cuando sea necesario para trabajar en modo mixto", to work autonomously, and, when the first campaign took hours, to reduce the number or the length of the simulations.
- Campaign (scripts in the session folder, not in the repository): each example file of EnergyPlus 25.1 with `People` (631; the 13 largest left out) prepared with its external files, three run periods in Seville and some outputs; simulated; if it worked, `AddAccis` with `ex_ac`, `ex_mm` (natural ventilation `ZoneVentilation` objects added to the occupied zones without it, alternately `WindandStackOpenArea` and `DesignFlowRate`), and both with all the `existing_hvac_*` options, simulated, and checked: errors of accim and EnergyPlus, severe errors that the original model does not have, whether the thermostats follow the setpoints of accim, the occupied time within the setpoints and, in mixed mode, whether EnergyPlus opens the windows that the EMS opens and only those (AirflowNetwork opening factors, or the air changes of the `ZoneVentilation` objects, against the ventilation hours of the EMS, timestep by timestep). Baseline (the code before, 10-day periods): 588 models whose original simulates, 2352 output IDFs: 46 errors of accim, 269 simulations stopped by EnergyPlus, 155 with new severe errors. After the fixes, 170 models chosen to save time (those that failed, at most two per kind of failure, those with natural ventilation and those that cover each kind of HVAC object; 5-day periods), 680 output IDFs: no error of accim; 662 complete; the 18 that stop are the two Kiva models (8), an evaporative cooler of a data center with `ex_mm` (2), a fan-powered box with all the options (1), the intermittent crash of EnergyPlus 25.1 (`HeatPumpVSAS`, below) and crashes of the parallel runs that simulate when run alone (6 of 7 checked one by one). In mixed mode, 1221 zones checked (62 with AirflowNetwork windows, 1159 with `ZoneVentilation` windows): the windows ventilate when the EMS opens them and only then, except where explained (hybrid ventilation managers kept with `MultizoneWithDistribution`; `MultizoneWithDistributionOnlyDuringFanOperation`, whose `ZoneVentilation` objects EnergyPlus only simulates while the fans are off; the fans of the models, `Intake`, `Exhaust` and `Balanced`, whose air changes are reported with those of the windows).
- Changes (problems found → fix):
  - Windows of mixed mode (`accim/sim/windows.py`, new; `model.py`): only DesignBuilder names were found (`..._Win`, `..._Door`, the zone before the first `_`; the doors between zones were opened as windows) → the exterior windows, doors and glass doors with AirflowNetwork openings (`afn_openings`), when the AirflowNetwork simulates the multizone airflows (`uses_airflow_network`; `NoMultizoneOrDistribution` was taken as AirflowNetwork); `ZoneVentilation:DesignFlowRate` gave a `BadEPFieldError` (field `Zone_or_Space_Name`) → the third field; the fans were windows → only `Natural`; ZoneLists and SpaceLists ignored → split per zone or space; the limits of the objects kept the windows closed (the OSM residential model of the golden master: maximum indoor temperature a schedule of 1 °C, its window never ventilated) → removed (`VENTILATION_LIMITS`); window names with `-` or spaces gave invalid EMS variables (`SPACE1-1_Win_OpT`) → `sanitize_ems_name`, unique; occupant ventilation controls (of the surface, or of the zone, which EnergyPlus gives to all the windows and doors of the zone, checked in `AirflowNetwork/src/Solver.cpp` of 25.1) and hybrid ventilation managers (which act on all the `ZoneVentilation` objects of the zones of their air loops) overrode the EMS → removed with a warning when accim controls windows, except the managers with `MultizoneWithDistribution` (without them, `AirflowNetwork_MultiZone_SmallOffice_VAV` stopped: "A diagonal entity is zero in AirflowNetwork matrix"); warnings for the zones without windows, the EMS actuators of the model on the windows and `MultizoneWithDistributionOnlyDuringFanOperation`.
  - `ex_mm` on the design days: the HVAC systems were off without occupants (usually the winter design day), EnergyPlus sized them for no load and stopped (`House-2FurnaceAC-*`: "air flow less than ...", water coils: "Autosizing of heating coil UA failed") → sensor `DesignDay` of the schedule `ACCIS Design Days` and lines at the end of `ApplyAST_<zone>` and `SetWindowOperation_<window>` (not wrapping the body: EnergyPlus allows 5 nested `IF`, and the window program has 5).
  - EMS of the models: `add_global_variables` and `add_internal_variables` replaced those of the model (33 example files stopped: "EMS variable not found among global variables", divisions by internal variables that no longer existed) → kept.
  - Thermostats: those of ZoneLists stopped accim (`ValueError`) → split per zone with their references (`ZoneControl:Thermostat:OperativeTemperature`, `:TemperatureAndHumidity`, `FaultModel:ThermostatOffset`, `DemandManager:Thermostats`), also the staged ones; with `existing_hvac_operative_temperature_control=True`, the thermal comfort thermostats (PMV) of the occupied zones are removed (`FurnaceWithDXSystemComfortControl`: 7 % within the setpoints, and EnergyPlus crashed) and the staged thermostats get the setpoint schedules of their zones (`MultiSpeedHP_StagedThermostat`: 0 to 0.6 % following them); with `False`, the setpoint schedules of all the occupied zones are added (EnergyPlus stopped: "Actuator AHST_ACT_... not found").
  - `HVACTemplate` models: accim did not see their thermostats, and the output IDFs kept the setpoints of the templates → `AddAccis` expands them (`accim.energyplus.expand_objects`, ExpandObjects with the IDD in its working folder); `add_accis_to_idf` stops with a `ValueError` for them.
  - Backup ideal loads (`existing_hvac_ideal_loads_backup=True`): its inlet node went into NodeLists that other objects use as single nodes (`SetpointManager:SingleZone:*`, `ZoneHVAC:IdealLoadsAirSystem`, `ZoneHVAC:VentilatedSlab`: "a Nodelist may not be valid in this context") → NodeLists of accim; it broke the models with AirflowNetwork distribution ("... is not defined as an AirflowNetwork:Distribution:Node object") and with the RoomAir model AirflowNetwork → not added there, with a warning.
  - `existing_hvac_availability_schedule` with VRF systems: `BadEPFieldError` (`obj.Name`) → `_object_name`.
  - Not accim: EnergyPlus 25.1 and 25.2 crash with the two Kiva models (`ZoneCoupledKivaConvection*`, adaptive convection algorithm) with operative temperature control, constant or scheduled; EnergyPlus 25.1 crashes at random with `HeatPumpVSAS` when the radiative fraction of the operative temperature thermostat is scheduled (3 of 5 runs, also with a schedule always 0.5; never with `Constant` nor with EnergyPlus 25.2): accim keeps its schedule. A `ZoneVentilation:WindandStackOpenArea` with height difference 0 gives no flow with the wind from behind (EnergyPlus 25.1, `ZoneEquipmentManager.cc`); the time within the setpoints of some models is limited by the models (zones without thermostats, controlled from another zone; capacity).
  - Documentation: `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md` (4.1, 4.1.1, new 4.1.2 on the windows), `docs/source/5_troubleshooting.md` (the crashes of EnergyPlus), `docs/source/9_migration_guide.md` (9.4), `docs/source/api/accim.sim.rst` (the new module, with `sphinx-apidoc` as in `docs/README.md` §6), `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.1.0.md` (section 8), `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/questions.md` (`vrf_mm` and the design days), `notes/work-log.md`.
- Files: `accim/sim/windows.py` (new), `accim/sim/model.py`, `accim/sim/hvac/existing.py`, `accim/sim/hvac/schedules.py`, `accim/sim/ems/objects.py`, `accim/sim/ems/program_lines.py`, `accim/sim/build.py`, `accim/sim/accis.py`, `accim/energyplus.py`; `tests/core/test_mixed_mode_windows.py` (new), `tests/core/test_existing_hvac.py`, `tests/core/test_existing_hvac_mixed_mode.py`, `tests/core/golden_master/golden/` (`b07` to `b10`, `b13`, `b15`, `b22`, `s04`); the documentation above.
- Verification: the campaigns above; new tests (11 of the windows, two of them simulations in which EnergyPlus opens the windows that the EMS opens and only those, timestep by timestep; 11 of the existing HVAC systems, one of them a simulation of `ex_mm` with the furnaces of `House-2FurnaceAC-SequentialLoad`; the design days with the Erl interpreter); golden master: only the `ex_mm` configurations (the design days), `b13` and `b15` (the limit schedules of the OSM residential model) and `b22` (the NodeLists of the backup) change, checked line by line, and the golden files were written again; `python -m pytest tests/core tests/parametric_and_optimisation -q -p no:cacheprovider`: 1314 passed; the documentation build gives the same warnings as before.
- Next step: the review of the author; the open question on `vrf_mm` and the design days (`notes/questions.md`).

### 2026-11-06
#### [done] EMS programs: only the code of the comfort standards of each output IDF, and indented lines (branch `v1.1.0`)
- Context: The author asked "sería posible que el código de EMS tuviera indentaciones cuando se genera el idf? [...] de forma que se pueda ver claramente dónde empiezan y terminan los bloques if-elif-else-endif" and "qué te parecería añadir únicamente el EMS program para las consignas especificadas, en lugar de añadir todas las líneas de todos los posible comfort_standard?". He was not available to choose among the options (the code of the comfort standards of each output IDF in all the programs that depend on it; only in the subprograms and `SetAST_Master`; or no change), so the recommended one was implemented, in the output IDFs of `AddAccis` only, for his review (`notes/questions.md`).
- Changes:
  - Indentation: `ems.programs.indent_lines` (4 spaces, `INDENTATION`, for each block `IF` or `WHILE`; the keyword is what comes before the first space, as in EnergyPlus; the spaces that a line had are replaced), used by `add_program` and `set_program_lines`. eppy strips the fields when it reads an IDF, so `indent_programs(idf)` indents all the programs and subroutines of a model: `AddAccis` calls it after reading the text of the model again, and `modify_accis` at its end.
  - The code of the comfort standards: `ems.program_lines.for_comfort_standards(lines, standards)` keeps the branches of some comfort standards of a chain `if ComfStand == ... endif` (the conditions only name them; `else` becomes an `elseif` of those that no condition names; a `ValueError` for other lines). `setast.master_program_lines(comfort_standards)`, `setast.subprograms(comfort_standards)` (the argument was `comf_stands`), `setast.subprogram_comfort_standards(idf)` and `inputs.zone_comfort_standards(idf)`. `programs.set_comfort_standard_code(idf, standards)` writes `SetComfTemp`, `SetAppLimits`, `ApplyCAT`, `CountHours_<zone>` and `SetAST_Master` from their templates and replaces the subprograms `SetAST_CS<n>_<chunk>` (at the end of the programs); a program without code for these comfort standards (`SetComfTemp` and `ApplyCAT` with ESP CTE and INT ISO7730) only returns, since EnergyPlus needs a line (`\min-fields 2`). `AddAccis._write_output_idfs` calls it before saving each output IDF, with the comfort standards of its zones; `modify_accis`, and `modify_ComfStand` of the parametric module (with an output IDF and `bypass_add_accis=True`, for instance), add the code of the comfort standards that they set if the model lacks it, and remove none (`programs.add_comfort_standard_code`); `add_accis_to_idf` keeps the code of all of them (the parametric module changes `ComfStand` in each sample).
  - Tests: new `tests/core/test_comfort_standard_code.py` (15 tests: the indentation; the chains; with the Erl compiler, the programs with the code of each comfort standard give the same variables as with that of all of them for every category and comfort mode, in 36 timesteps with RMOT and PMOT different; a comfort standard without its code stops; two zones with different comfort standards; `modify_accis` and `modify_ComfStand`). `test_zone_arguments.py` checks the code and the indentation of the output IDFs; `test_modifyaccis_setast.py` and `test_space_instance_names.py` compare indented lines.
  - Documentation: `CHANGELOG.md` (Unreleased), the migration guide (9.4), the detailed use (4.2: the comfort standard of an output IDF is changed with `modify_accis`), the docstrings of `AddAccis`, `add_accis_to_idf` and `modify_accis`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.1.0.md` (section 7), the text of the source notebook `full_example_IBPSA`, `TODO.md` (regenerate two How-to pages), `notes/decisions.md` and `notes/questions.md`.
- Files: `accim/sim/accis.py`, `accim/sim/ems/programs.py`, `accim/sim/ems/program_lines.py`, `accim/sim/ems/setast.py`, `accim/sim/ems/inputs.py`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/funcs_for_besos/param_accis.py`; `tests/core/test_comfort_standard_code.py` (new), `tests/core/test_zone_arguments.py`, `tests/core/test_modifyaccis_setast.py`, `tests/core/test_space_instance_names.py`, the 22 golden files `b*.txt.xz`; the documentation above.
- Verification:
  - An output IDF of the sample model (`vrf_mm`, two zones, INT EN16798): 78 EMS programs with 6851 lines (645 KB) before, 34 with 1393 lines (357 KB) now.
  - The test of the Erl compiler fails if the branch `else` is left out or if the hours of a comfort standard with PMOT are counted with the branch of RMOT (checked by changing `for_comfort_standards`); with RMOT and PMOT equal, as in its first version, it missed the second.
  - Golden master: the 22 configurations of `AddAccis` change. In their 424 output IDFs, a script checked that the other objects are identical, that only subprograms of comfort standards that the zones do not use are removed (18434), and that the chains and `SetAST_Master` are the old ones cut by `for_comfort_standards`; the 9 configurations of the in-memory functions do not change (the comparison ignores the spaces). The golden files were written again.
  - EnergyPlus: identical CSV results for 19 pairs of output IDFs (before: the golden files or a worktree of `5f133cb0`; now: this code): 13 of the golden master (`b01` with ESP CTE, CHN GBT50785 Cold, AUS Williamson with comfort mode 0.1, MEX Oropeza Arid and INT ISO7730; `b02`; `b04`, PMV; `b07`, `ex_mm`; `b11`, OSM office; `b15`, OSM residential; `b16`, `b17` and `b18`, with EnergyPlus 26.2), one of INT EN16798 with `vrf_mm`, two with `zone_arguments` (comfort standards 0 and 13, 1 and 13), the custom model with valid coefficients, and the sample models of EnergyPlus 9.4 and 9.6. The custom model with the default coefficients of the golden master stops in the sizing, before and now. Simulations run in parallel need a working folder each: ReadVarsESO writes `readvars.audit` in the working folder, and EnergyPlus ends with a fatal error when another run removes it.
  - Speed: `AddAccis` with the 140 output IDFs of the sample model, in interleaved runs: 15.5 to 16.4 s now, 16.0 to 17.0 s before (`5f133cb0`), 4.7 to 5.8 s with 1.0.0.
  - `tests/core` and `tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 1292 passed in 9 min 41 s. The numbered tests of the parametric module (EnergyPlus 9.6, models of `add_accis_to_idf` with indented programs): 87 passed in 37 min (at the same time as the other tests); `08_test_accim_predef_model.py` again after the last change of `modify_ComfStand`: 4 passed. The documentation builds with the same 59 lines of warnings, none from the changed modules or pages.
- Next step: the review of the author (`notes/questions.md`); regenerate the How-to pages `full_example_IBPSA` and `using_apmv_setpoints` before the release (`TODO.md`).

#### [done] `accim.sim` restructured around eppy (branch `v1.1.0`)
- Context: The author asked for "otra reestructuración de accim, usando, cuando sea posible, eppy", mainly in `accim.sim`, in a new branch `v1.1.0`, "lo más fácil y simple de entender"; on seeing that eppy is slower, he preferred it "más entendible aunque sea un poco más lento", and eppy rather than besos "siempre que podamos" (decision in `notes/decisions.md`). Branch `v1.1.0` from `master` (`73f958b5`).
- Changes:
  - Reading: `accim.sim.accis.load_idf` reads every model with eppy, also with `'auto'` (the version of the `Version` object of the file, `idd_path`, and `find_energyplus` for a `FileNotFoundError` with the folders searched), and stops with a `ValueError` for a model of another version once eppy has an IDD; `_set_idd`, `_idd_version` and `_check_besos_idd` removed. `Table`, `chile_funcs.apply_heating_activation_time_sch` (which imported besos with the module), `verify_accim_simulation`, `AccimSimulationVerifier`, `modify_timesteps_path`, `set_occupancy_to_always_path` and `set_operative_temp_control(idf_path=...)` use it instead of `besos.eppy_funcs.get_building`. besos stays in the parametric module and in `accim.utils.outputs` (it runs EnergyPlus and reads the ESO); `accim.utils.get_building` is still that of besos.
  - Writing: `generation.IdfWriter` and `_save_text` removed; `generation.set_combination(idf, args, combination, zone_arguments)` sets the inputs of each zone (`set_program_lines` with `input_lines`), and `AddAccis._write_output_idfs` saves each output IDF with `IDF.savecopy`. `AddAccis` reads each input file twice (`input_idfs`, and the model that gets the ACCIS; `copy_idf` removed) and keeps `idf.idfstr()` of each model, which `IDF(StringIO(text))` reads again (an eppy model of the sample takes about 20 MB, its text 0.14 MB).
  - Editing: `accim/sim/idf_tools.py` removed. `try_objects` → `idf.idfobjects[...]` (the types that all the IDDs 9.1 to 26.2 have) or `idf.idfobjects.get('SPACE', [])` (Space and SpaceList since 9.6, People:Instance and People:Definition since 26.2, the radiant `:Design` objects since 9.6; checked in the IDDs of 9.0.1, 9.4 and 9.6; with besos, `.get` needs the key in capitals); `remove_objects` → `removeallidfobjects`; `add_program` and `set_program_lines` (which deletes `fieldvalues[2:]` and sets each line by its field name, so that eppy extends the fields of the IDD) in `ems/programs.py`; `.obj` → `fieldvalues`/`key` in `ems/inputs.py`, `ems/programs.py`, `ems/objects.py`, `hvac/schedules.py`, `hvac/existing.py`, `utils/people.py` and `utils/accim_args.py`; the existence checks with `IDF.getobject` (case-insensitive) in `model.py`, `ems/objects.py`, `ems/programs.add_programs`, `hvac/vrf.py`, `hvac/schedules.add_compact_schedule`, `hvac/existing.py` (availability managers, node lists, equipment lists, radiant design objects), `apmv.py` and `utils/people.get_people_definition`. One `ems.objects.add_sensor` for the sensors of `ems/objects`, `hvac/vrf` and `hvac/existing`; `existing._add_schedule` → `add_compact_schedule`; `existing._zone_lists` shared; the probe of the names of the limits of the VRF outdoor units, once instead of once per zone.
  - Python API backend: `energyplus_api/idf.py` rewritten: `read_ems(idf)` with the field names of eppy, and `prepare_for_api(idf)` (removes the programs, subroutines, calling managers and actuators with `removeallidfobjects`, changes the EMS output variables of programs, adds the global variable); `parse_idf`, `read_idf`, `render_idf`, `objects_for_api` and `IdfObject` removed. `runner.run_simulation` reads the version with `read_idf_version` (so the check of the API version comes first), the model with `load_idf`, and saves it with `saveas`. `tools/compare_backends.py` shortens the run period with eppy.
  - `apmv.py`: the programs as lists of lines through `add_program`, the lines of the cooling season shared with `SetAST_Master` (`ems/setast.COOLING_SEASON_LINES`), the four blocks of `set_pmv_input_parameters` as one loop. `chile_funcs.py`: the 130 fields of the schedule from `_MONTHS` (identical to the old ones, checked), `load_idf`, one `savecopy`.
  - Found on the way: `existing._outdoor_temperature_limits` read each limit with `str(value or '')`, so a limit of 0 °C given as a number (as eppy reads it) was taken as the default of the IDD; fixed.
  - Tests: `test_generation.py` (`set_combination` instead of `IdfWriter`), `test_energyplus_api.py` (the models read with eppy through `_idf()`, the run periods set by their field names), `test_load_idf_versions.py` (the message of eppy, and a new test that `load_idf` gives the `IDF` of eppy), `test_chile_funcs.py` (new), `test_existing_hvac.py` (the limit of 0 °C, new; `idfstr`), `test_modifyaccis_setast.py`, the fakes of `test_people_instance.py`, `test_space_instance_names.py`, `test_table_columns.py`, `test_table_spaces.py`, `test_utils_idf_hierarchy.py` and `test_zone_arguments.py` (`getobject`, `.get`, `fieldvalues`), and the tests of `Table`, which patch `accim.sim.accis.load_idf` instead of `get_building`; the golden files of 19 configurations written again.
  - Documentation: `CHANGELOG.md` (Unreleased), the migration guide (9.4, new), the detailed use (4.1, eppy; the aPMV example with `load_idf`; `run_simulation`), the troubleshooting (the IDD of the session), `docs/source/api/accim.sim.rst` (without `idf_tools`), `tests/core/golden_master/README.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.1.0.md` (new, with a pointer in the 1.0.0 guide), `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
- Files: `accim/sim/` (`accis.py`, `apmv.py`, `chile_funcs.py`, `generation.py`, `model.py`, `outputs.py`, `people.py`, `ems/inputs.py`, `ems/objects.py`, `ems/programs.py`, `ems/setast.py`, `energyplus_api/__init__.py`, `energyplus_api/idf.py`, `energyplus_api/runner.py`, `hvac/existing.py`, `hvac/schedules.py`, `hvac/vrf.py`; `idf_tools.py` removed), `accim/utils/` (`accim_args.py`, `editing.py`, `people.py`, `verification.py`), `accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`, `tools/compare_backends.py`, 20 test files and `tests/core/golden_master/golden/` (19 files), `docs/source/` (`4_detailed use.md`, `5_troubleshooting.md`, `9_migration_guide.md`, `api/accim.sim.rst`), `CHANGELOG.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.0.0.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.1.0.md`, `DEVLOG.md`, `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
- Verification:
  - Baseline on `master`: 1273 passed in 10 min 24 s.
  - Golden master: 12 configurations did not change: those of the in-memory functions (`s01` to `s09`, whose models the golden master reads with besos), `b15` (with an explicit version, read with eppy before too) and `b13` and `b14` (OpenStudio models, whose numbers are already as eppy writes them); the other 19 differed only in 2926 numeric fields with the same values (compared field by field: `0.0`→`0` 814, `2.0`→`2` 798, `1.0`→`1` 405, `0.00000001`→`1e-08` 399, `3.0`→`3` 399, `-0`→`0` 36, the glare angle of the `Daylighting:Controls` of the OSM office...). The first output IDF of `b02`, `b11` and `b17` (EnergyPlus 25.1 and 26.2), as in the golden files and as written now, simulated for 3 days with Seville.epw: identical CSV files. Then the golden files were written again.

  - The new test of the limit of 0 °C fails with the code before (the old line restored for the check).

### 2026-11-03
#### [done] Sessions saved in a pickle file, to resume them, and a JSON file, to read them (Table, accim.utils, the GUI)
- Context: The author asked that the classic workflow (as in the notebook of the research paper case study: `rename_epw_files`, `AddAccis`, `run_ep`, `Table`) could save a pickle file to resume the session, "as the parametric module already does", also in the GUI, and additionally a JSON file that people can read. Asked to choose, he took both `Table.save`/`Table.load` and `save_session`/`load_session`, and in the GUI the whole session (the forms of all the tabs, the table, the hours and the parametric results) plus opening in the tab *Table and figures* the tables saved in Python. The results of `run_ep` stay in the CSV files, but the tables were lost with the session: the hourly table of the 4 sample CSV files takes about 9 s to make (its pickle, 0.06 s to write and 0.1 s to read).
- Changes:
  - `accim/utils/session.py` (new; `save_session` and `load_session` exported lazily by `accim.utils`): `save_session(filename, /, *, json_max_rows=1000, **objects)` writes `<filename>.pkl` (`pd.to_pickle` of `{'accim_session': 1, 'accim_version', 'saved', 'folder', 'objects'}`) and then `<filename>.json` (a description, the metadata and each object as `{'type', 'value'}`; `_json_value`: the tables of `Table` through their `_json_content`, the DataFrames and Series as `{'number_of_rows', 'rows_shown', 'data'}` with their first `json_max_rows` rows and their index unless it is the default one (pandas 3 turns `iloc[[0, 2]]` into `RangeIndex(0, 4, 2)`, which is not), the columns of several levels joined with '[', the numbers of numpy, dates and paths, NaN as null and infinity as 'Infinity' (strict JSON: `allow_nan=False`, `ensure_ascii=False`), and other objects as their `repr`). Each file is written through a temporary one (`os.replace`), the pickle first (if the JSON fails, a `RuntimeError` says that the pickle file was saved), and an object that pickle cannot save is named in a `TypeError` (`_check_picklable`), with no file written. `load_session` returns the objects by name and prints when, with which version and where they were saved; the JSON file and the tables of `Table.save` get hints.
  - `Table` (`accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`): `arguments` (the arguments of `__init__`, from `locals()` at its start), `type_of_table`/`custom_cols` of the last successful `format_table` (`'all'` while it runs and after a failure; `custom_cols` is read once, so generators work), `_JSON_ATTRIBUTES`, `_json_content`, `save(filename='accim_table', json_max_rows=1000)` (pickles the table itself; a table of `concatenated_csv_name` has nothing to save) and the classmethod `load(pickle_path)` (`.pkl` can be left out).
  - GUI: `tasks.SESSION_FORMS` (`'gui_forms'`), `table_format`, `save_table`, `open_table` (formats the table again as it was), `save_session` and `open_session` (rejects the sessions saved in Python); `app.py`: the menu *Session* (*Open a session...*, *Save the session...*), `state()`/`restore()` in every tab (fields, check buttons, selections of the lists, tables of the zones and of the parameters, texts of the cities and countries), `HoursTab.shown`/`clear` (`show` takes the zones), `TableTab.save_table`/`open_table`/`clear` (the buttons *Save the table...* and *Open a table...*; the figures make the table again with `tasks.table_format` instead of the former `table_type`), `ParametricTab.clear_results`, and `AccimApp.tabs`/`session_objects`/`save_session`/`open_session`/`restore_session` (the folder first, so that the lists have the files of the folder before their selections are restored; the list of the comfort standards is enabled to restore its selection with PMV setpoints; the files that are not in the folder are listed in the log; a session without results clears those of the window). The paths are made absolute before the tasks, which run in the folder of the file.
  - The notebook of the research paper case study: section 3.3.3 (`dataset_runperiod.save`, `save_session` with both tables and the lists of files, `load_session` and `Table.load` to resume them, and the keys of the JSON file), before the cleanup, which deletes these files too; its page was regenerated.

- Context: The author answered the five P2 items of `TODO.md`: the radiant systems, ventilated slabs and cooling panels follow the setpoints, "but as one or several True/False arguments that apply the change or not, in line with the previous decisions" (he chose `True` as the default when asked); the GUI shows the `existing_hvac_*` arguments (with `ex_ac` and `ex_mm`); `sampling_full_set()` keeps one row of ESP CTE for each combination of the other parameters (the first value of `category` and `comfort_mode`); he checked the formulas of CHN Yang `(6, 2)` and `(6, 3)` against the source and corrected 2 lines of `(6, 2)`; and he installed EnergyPlus 26.1 for the simulations. - Changes:
  - Radiant systems (`existing_hvac_radiant_setpoints=True`, `hvac/existing.set_radiant_setpoints`): the objects of `_RADIANT_FIELDS` in occupied zones (the zone of a cooling panel is that of the `ZoneHVAC:EquipmentList` that has it, through `ZoneHVAC:EquipmentConnections`) get `AHST_Sch_<zone>`/`ACST_Sch_<zone>` (added if missing, `_add_schedule`) in the setpoint fields that are not blank, so EnergyPlus keeps deciding whether they heat or cool from their nodes, flows and schedules; the control temperature becomes `OperativeTemperature` (`OperativeTemperatureSetpoint` for the types `...Setpoint`). Variable flow: the control temperature schedules and type of its `:Design` object (since 9.6; in the main object before), copied as '<zone> ACCIS <design>' when systems of several zones share it (`_design_object`). Constant flow and ventilated slabs: both the high and the low control temperature of each mode are the setpoint (on/off at it; EnergyPlus accepts equal values: `LowTempRadiantSystem.cc` and `VentilatedSlab.cc` of 25.1 divide by the difference only when it is not 0, and stop only when high < low). Electric and high temperature: the heating setpoint. Cooling panels: in a zone with a thermostat (also through a `ZoneList`, `_thermostat_zones`), the control type `ZoneTotalLoad`, because their operative temperature control gave 420 "Plant flows do not resolve" severe errors in `5ZoneCoolingPanelBaseboard` (isolated: the control type, not the schedule); without a thermostat, the cooling setpoint schedule; the panels already controlled by the load are skipped. The step runs before the thermostats, so that the zones whose radiant systems follow the setpoints (`model.radiant_zones`) are left out of the warning about zones without a thermostat. `False` keeps the warning (`warn_own_setpoints`). ### 2026-11-02
#### [done] The existing HVAC systems keep the operative temperature between the setpoints (arguments `existing_hvac_*`)
- Context: The author had seen that, with some models and their existing HVAC systems, the operative temperature did not follow the adaptive setpoints and exceeded them, and had used the VRF systems of accim instead; he asked whether the existing equipment could keep it between them (related to `ex_mm`), and then to investigate the HVAC systems themselves (capacity, cooling that stops above an outdoor temperature, which he had fixed in the VRF systems), for any IDF and HVAC system, with arguments that simulate the model as it is when they are off. He chose: the operative temperature control behind an argument enabled by default, the availability as an option with a warning, and the names `existing_hvac_operative_temperature_control` and `existing_hvac_availability_schedule` (the other two follow them; decision in `notes/decisions.md`).
- Investigation (year in Seville, EnergyPlus 25.1, timestep outputs, INT EN16798-1 category II, comfort mode 3, tolerance 0.1; percentage of the occupied time with the operative temperature between the setpoints without tolerance, per zone): (1) the existing thermostats controlled the air temperature, and the VRF systems of accim the operative temperature (`ZoneControl:Thermostat:OperativeTemperature`, `TypOperativeTempControlSch`): the sample OSM office had 53 to 77 % (86 to 93 % with operative temperature control); the sample models of DesignBuilder already had it (PTACs 100 %, beams 99.9 %; without it, 78 and 31 to 35 %); only 3 of the 619 example files of EnergyPlus have it. (2) accim only gave the setpoints to the first control of each thermostat: 287 of the 619 example files have thermostats with a single cooling, a single heating and a dual setpoint object shared by all the zones, and with `5ZoneAirCooled` the dual one, which the control type schedule selects, kept the setpoints of the model, and EnergyPlus stopped (the `AHST_Sch_*` schedules of the actuators were missing). (3) Availability: the OSM office is off from 19 to 23 h (operation schedule and night cycle) while occupied: 100 % with operative temperature control and always available (99.9 to 100 % with `ex_mm`). (4) The HVAC system itself: the VAV terminals of `5ZoneAirCooled` deliver at most 3 kW against a load of 8 kW (winter afternoons of SPACE1-1), and their reheat at the minimum flow cannot meet the heating of the summer mornings (SPACE5-1); the radiators cannot cool (27 to 32 %); the outdoor temperature limits (VRF in EnergyPlus: cooling from -6 to 43 °C, heating from -20 to 16 °C; the OSM heat pumps: compressor down to -12.2 °C and gas heaters only up to 4.44 °C) did not matter in Seville. (5) In mixed mode, the rest of the hours out of the setpoints are timesteps with the windows open (air from 23 to 5 °C in 10 minutes in the PTAC model) and the first hour after the unoccupied periods. (6) Radiant systems, ventilated slabs and cooling panels follow setpoint schedules of their own, and plant operation schemes with one seasonal scheme switch off the plant equipment.
- Changes: `hvac/existing.py`: `set_operative_temperature_thermostats` (`existing_hvac_operative_temperature_control=True`, the default with temperature setpoints): the thermostat of each occupied zone uses only a `ThermostatSetpoint:DualSetpoint` of its own (one it had, or '<zone> Dual SP', if no other thermostat uses it; otherwise a new one) with `AHST_Sch_<zone>`/`ACST_Sch_<zone>`, `Control type schedule: Always 4`, and a `ZoneControl:Thermostat:OperativeTemperature` with `TypOperativeTempControlSch` if it has none (an adaptive comfort model of EnergyPlus in it is set to None); the zones without a thermostat get their schedules with a warning; a `ZoneList` thermostat with occupied zones raises a `ValueError`; `False` runs `add_setpoint_schedules` as before. `make_available` (`existing_hvac_availability_schedule`, `None` by default, with `warn_availability`): the field 'Availability Schedule Name' of the HVAC objects that heat or cool (zone equipment, air terminals, unitary systems, fans, coils, coil systems, VRF, evaporative coolers; not exhaust fans, ERV, outdoor air units, DOAS, dehumidifiers), the operation schemes objects with one scheme, `AvailabilityManager:Scheduled` 'ACCIS Availability Manager' for the air and plant loops with availability managers, no managers for the zone equipment. `widen_outdoor_temperature_limits` (`existing_hvac_widen_outdoor_temperature_limits`, `False`, with `warn_outdoor_temperature_limits`): the fields 'Minimum/Maximum Outdoor ... Temperature' and 'Minimum/Maximum Condenser Inlet Node Temperature in ... Mode' (not defrost, crankcase heaters, heat recovery mode) widened to -20 and 80 °C within the bounds of the IDD. `add_ideal_loads_backups` (`existing_hvac_ideal_loads_backup`, `False`): '<zone> ACCIS Backup Ideal Loads', last in the cooling and heating sequences (`SequentialLoad`), own inlet and exhaust nodes (added to the NodeLists of the zone, or to new ones), no limits, no outdoor air, no humidity control; outputs in `outputs.py` (`model.ideal_loads_backups`). `warn_own_setpoints`: the radiant systems, ventilated slabs and cooling panels. New `hvac/schedules.py`: the schedules shared with the VRF systems (`add_compact_schedule`, `add_radiative_fraction_schedule`, from `vrf.py`) and the values of the schedules of a model (`schedule_values`, `is_always_on`: Constant, Compact, Year/Week/Day, without the design days). - Context: The second priority task asked by the author (2026-11-01): `ex_mm` was "UNDER DEVELOPMENT"; it found one heating and one cooling object of each zone (`hvac/resolver.py`: the zone field, the zone equipment, the air loops, the names; or `hvac_zone_map`) and read their coils (`<zone>_CoolCoil`, `<zone>_HeatCoil`, initialised by `InitExisHVACCoils`). A baseline simulation of the four sample models with existing HVAC systems (golden master inputs, `ex_mm`, HVACmode 2, Seville, timestep outputs): PTACs worked; the electric radiators stopped EnergyPlus (`BLOCK1_ZONE2_COOLCOIL` not initialised: no cooling object); the OSM office was rejected (two kinds of heating coils); with the cooled beams, the windows were open in 13 and 44 timesteps in which the thermostat had been asking for cooling. The output `Zone Air System Sensible Heating/Cooling Rate` was not usable as the state (the DOAS of the beams keeps it above 0 in every timestep, and it does not count the heat of the radiators).
- Changes: `ex_mm` reads, for each zone and window, the load to the setpoints that the thermostat of the zone asks for (`Zone Predicted Sensible Load to Setpoint Heat Transfer Rate`, 0 between the setpoints): `hvac/existing.add_load_sensors` (sensors `<zone>_LoadToSetpoint`, `<window>_LoadToSetpoint`, keyed by the zone of each target and window), and the programs `ApplyAST_<zone>` and `SetWindowOperation_<window>` of `ex_mm` start with `if <name>_LoadToSetpoint == 0` instead of the two coil conditions (`program_lines`: the shared bodies, `_HVAC_OFF_FROM_COILS` and `_HVAC_OFF_FROM_LOAD`, and the constants `..._EXISTING_HVAC`; `programs.accis_programs` chooses by script type). Removed: `scan_existing_hvac`, the coil sensors, `InitExisHVACCoils`, `ExistingHvacGroup` and `hvac/resolver.py`. `hvac_zone_map` is deprecated and has no effect (`DeprecationWarning` in `AddAccis` and `add_accis_to_idf`). ### 2026-11-01
#### [done] The hours within the setpoints: corrected counters, their percentage and a filter of the simulations
- Context: The author asked (2026-11-01, priority task of `TODO.md`) that the hours within the setpoints (with the EMS output of the comfortable hours, or similar) be easy to measure, to filter out the simulations below a percentage. A baseline simulation of the sample model (four configurations, three weeks of Seville) showed that `Occupied Comfortable Hours_No Applicability_<zone>` exceeded `Occupied Hours_<zone>` (up to 107 % with natural ventilation): `CountHoursNoApp_<zone>` did not set it out of the setpoints, so it kept the value of the previous timestep. The two problems of `CountHours_<zone>` found earlier (no counter below the heating setpoint within the applicability limits; the setpoints of the previous timestep) were confirmed. The P2 task of `make_averages` (`b02`, `b17`) came from the same counters.
- Changes: `COUNT_HOURS` and `COUNT_HOURS_NO_APP` set all their counters in every timestep (one of the five with applicability is the timestep; the occupied comfortable and discomfortable hours add up to the occupied hours), `RunACCIS_<zone>` runs `CountHours_<zone>` after `SetASTnoTol`, and `ACSTnoTol`/`AHSTnoTol` moved from `ZONE_STATE_VARIABLES` (no program reads them before they are set now) to `ZONE_RESULT_VARIABLES`; `APPLY_AST_MIXED_MODE` sets `VentHours_<zone>` to 0 with `HVACmode` 0. New module `accim.data.postprocessing.setpoint_hours`: `hours_within_setpoints()` (CSV files, folders, or a table with one row per simulation: results of the parametric module, `Table.df`) and `filter_by_hours_within_setpoints()` (building, every zone, or a list of zones). `Table`: the block and building totals no longer add up the columns of longer outputs that contain the name of the output (the occupied counters in `Comfortable Hours_No Applicability`) or the building averages of `make_averages` (`_of_output`). Design: the percentage is computed in Python from the two existing counters (exact at any reporting frequency, and the building is the ratio of the sums of the zones), instead of a new EMS output, whose hourly averages would mix the occupied and unoccupied timesteps; in an optimisation, the sum of the occupied discomfortable hours works as a constraint (decision in `notes/decisions.md`).
- Files: `accim/sim/ems/program_lines.py`, `accim/sim/ems/programs.py`, `accim/data/postprocessing/setpoint_hours.py` (new), `accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`, `tests/core/test_hour_counters.py` (new), `tests/core/test_setpoint_hours.py` (new), `tests/core/test_table_columns.py`, `tests/core/test_zone_arguments.py`, `tests/core/test_energyplus_versions.py`, `tests/core/golden_master/golden/*` (26 files), `docs/source/4_detailed use.md` (4.7.1), `docs/source/parametric_and_optimisation.rst`, `docs/source/9_migration_guide.md` (9.3), `docs/source/api/accim.data.postprocessing.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.0.0.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification: the sample model simulated before and after (vrf_ac, vrf_mm with `HVACmode` 0, 1 and 2; Seville, three weeks): after, no row where the occupied comfortable and discomfortable hours differ from the occupied hours (before, up to 67 of 600), nor where the five counters with applicability do not add up to one hour, and the percentages within the setpoints went from 100-107 % to 79-98 %; no other column changed (setpoints, temperatures, energy). `b02` (`HVACmode` 0) and `b17` (EnergyPlus 26.2) with `make_averages` simulate. The tests of the counters fail with the old programs, and the test of the totals of `Table` with the old `Table`. The golden master changed only in `CountHours_*`, `CountHoursNoApp_*`, `ApplyAST_*`, `RunACCIS_*`, `InitASTvariables` and the global variables (checked object by object) and was written again. The example of the parametric documentation run end to end (one IDF of the tests, `HVACmode` 0 and 1): 99.1 % and 42.6 % of the occupied hours of the building within the setpoints, and the filter at 90 % keeps `HVACmode` 0. `python -m pytest tests/core tests/parametric_and_optimisation -q -p no:cacheprovider`: 1012 passed.

#### [done] The arguments of each zone (`zone_arguments`), in one EMS program per zone
- Context: The author asked (2026-11-01) that the user can give the arguments of the ACCIS for each zone or space, that the inputs of the EMS be in one program (`SetInputData`) per zone, and a review of the names of the arguments with suggestions (in `notes/questions.md`). He chose the options: `zone_arguments` in `AddAccis` and `modify_accis`; outputs of each zone besides those of the whole model; `modify_accis` still working with the models of earlier versions, without arguments per zone. Decision in `notes/decisions.md`.
- Changes:
  - New `accim/sim/ems/inputs.py`: `INPUT_VARIABLES` (the 26 inputs of `SetInputData_<zone>`: the 12 of `SetInputData` and those of `SetVOFinputData`, `ApplyCAT`, `SetAppLimits` and `SetAST`, with the argument and default of each one), `input_lines`, `input_values`, `input_programs`, `read_inputs`, `set_inputs` (by variable name), `check_zone_arguments`, `zone_names` and `resolve_zone_arguments` (zones by name, key of the aPMV setpoints or EMS name, in any case).
  - `ems/programs.py`: `SetInputData_<zone>` and `RunACCIS_<zone>` for each zone. `RunACCIS_<zone>` runs `SetInputData_<zone>`, restores the variables that the shared programs read before setting them (`ZONE_STATE_VARIABLES`: `ACST`, `AHST`, `ACSTnoTol`, `AHSTnoTol`, `ComfTemp`, `CoolingSeason`), runs `SetAppLimits`, `ApplyCAT`, `SetComfTemp`, `CountHours_<zone>`, `SetAST`, `SetASTnoTol`, `CountHoursNoApp_<zone>`, `SetVST`, `ApplyAST_<zone>` and the programs of the windows of the zone (the order of 0.x), and stores those variables and the setpoints of the zone (`roundedACST_<zone>`...). `InitASTvariables` initialises them at each new environment (the setpoints as Null). Only `SetGeoVar<zone>`, `RunACCIS_<zone>` and `InitASTvariables` (and the windows outside the occupied zones) have calling managers. `program_lines.py`: `SetAppLimits` and `ApplyCAT` read the custom model from `CustAST_*`, `ApplyCAT` no longer sets `CATcoolOffset` and `CATheatOffset`, `SetAST` only runs `SetAST_Master`; `SetInputData` and `SetVOFinputData` removed.
  - `ems/objects.py`: global variables of the new inputs and of each zone; outputs of the setpoints of each zone (`ZONE_SETPOINT_OUTPUT_VARIABLES`).
  - `generation.py`: `IdfWriter` writes the programs `SetInputData_<zone>` of each combination (`zone_input_arguments`, with the zones of `zone_arguments`); `zone_arguments_errors`. `accis.py`: `AddAccis(zone_arguments=...)` (checked before writing any output IDF, attribute `zone_arguments`), `modify_accis(zone_arguments=...)`, `_modify_accis_of_earlier_versions` (the previous code, for the models with one `SetInputData`).
  - Consumers: `param_accis.py` (the setters set a variable in all the zones, or the lines of the models of earlier versions, `_EARLIER_VERSION_LINES`; new `get_custom_model_arguments`, used by `main.py`, whose unused `import accim` was removed), `accim/utils/accim_args.py` (`get_accim_args` per zone), `verification.py` (fallback to `SetInputData_<zone>`), the deprecated class of `accis_single_idf` (`zone_input_programs`; no lines appended to `SetAST`), the sample script `accis_for_single_idf_usage.py`.
  - Docs: `docs/source/4_detailed use.md` (section 4.2.1 and the table of arguments), the migration guide (9.3), `docs/source/parametric_and_optimisation.rst`, `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.0.0.md` and the CHANGELOG.

#### [done] The last two recommendations: Pareto status with constraints, identity of the checkpoints, comfort objectives
- Context: The author chose the recommendations of `notes/questions.md` for the integrity block of `review/refactor-sim-v1-20261024` and for a comfort objective independent of the control (decision records in `notes/decisions.md`; no question is open now).
- Changes:
  - New `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/_pareto.py` (adapted from that branch): `non_dominated_mask` compares the evaluations as the `ParetoDominance` of platypus (a smaller total violation of the constraints dominates a larger one; the objectives decide between equal violations), and the evaluations without finite values are never non-dominated and dominate none. `_annotate_pareto_status` uses it (by position, so repeated index labels work; with a warning if the columns of the constraints are missing), and so does the cleanup of the workers with `keep_sim_files='non-dominated'` (`patches._patched_eval_func`), which keeps the folders of the evaluations without finite values.
  - Checkpoints: `_build_parametric_task_signature(content=...)` gets the SHA-256 of the IDF of each task (its objects, without comments or spaces: `_content_digest(idf=True)`) and of its EPW, and the names of the outputs, computed once for each file in `_iter_parametric_task_blueprints`; a resumed run says how many tasks of the checkpoint it does not reuse. The preflight report counts the repeated rows of the plan as before.
  - Comfort objectives: `objectives.hours_outside_comfort` (hourly values: the sum of 1 - value over the values from 0 to 1) and `fraction_outside_comfort` (hourly or timestep values: their mean), which check that the values are a comfort status (-1 to 1) and their frequency. The overview page of the parametric module shows how to add the status variable (`set_output_variables_to_idf(..., validate=False)`: `select_outputs` only finds the variables that the model requests) and read it.
  - Docs: the overview page of the parametric module (constraints, long runs, comfort objectives), the migration guide (9.3), `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.0.0.md` (2, 10) and the CHANGELOG.
- Files: `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/_pareto.py` (new), `main.py`, `patches.py`, `objectives.py`; `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_constraint_pareto.py` and `test_parametric_checkpoint_identity.py` (new), `test_objectives.py`, `test_parametric_batch_checkpoint.py`; `docs/source/parametric_and_optimisation.rst`, `docs/source/9_migration_guide.md`; `CAMBIOS_CODIGO_v1.0.0.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`, `notes/decisions.md`, `notes/questions.md`, `notes/branch-reviews.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
- Verification:
  - New tests: `test_constraint_pareto.py` (21: the Pareto status equals the front of the `ParetoDominance` of platypus for 9 problems with the bounds `<=`, `>=`, `==`, `<`, `!=` and none; groups with repeated labels; values that are not finite; missing columns of constraints; and the cleanup of the workers), `test_parametric_checkpoint_identity.py` (5) and 8 more in `test_objectives.py`, one of them a simulation of 7 days with EnergyPlus 25.1. #### [done] Optimisation algorithms of `run_optimisation()`
- Context: TODO P2: PAES and ParticleSwarm could never run, and several algorithms failed with the defaults of `run_optimisation()`. Checking each algorithm of platypus 1.4.1 on small problems (real and categorical parameters, populations of 2 to 5, one step at a time) found more than the TODO said: platypus takes the size of the generations of OMOPSO and SMPSO from `swarm_size` and of CMAES, GeneticAlgorithm and EvolutionaryStrategy from `offspring_size` (100 by default), and ignores `population_size` for the first three (`Algorithm` swallows unknown keyword arguments), so these optimisations ran 100 simulations per generation; EvolutionaryStrategy, like PAES, takes one parent, and besos gives every algorithm a two-parent crossover; OMOPSO fails with categorical parameters at its first iteration; NSGAIII only minimises and takes its population from `divisions_outer`. The smoke test of the 16 algorithms hid these failures, because with `evaluations=2` most algorithms stopped after the initial population.
- Changes: `_check_optimisation_algorithm()` raises clear errors before anything is written (`ParticleSwarm`, with the advice to use OMOPSO or SMPSO; unknown names; `epsilons` of EpsMOEA, OMOPSO and EpsNSGAII; `divisions_outer` and maximised objectives with NSGAIII; SPEA2 with a population below k + 2; categorical parameters with OMOPSO, SMPSO and CMAES; single-objective algorithms with several objectives). The dispatch of the 16 `if` branches is one call: `population_size` is also passed as `swarm_size` or `offspring_size`, PAES gets no `population_size`, PAES and EvolutionaryStrategy get the mutation of besos as their variator, and `algorithm_options` overrides all of them. The docstring of `run_optimisation()` documents the requirements and the meaning of `population_size` for each algorithm. Decision record in `notes/decisions.md`.
- Files: `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_optimisation_algorithms.py` (new), `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/04_test_optimization.py`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/README.md`, `docs/source/9_migration_guide.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`, `notes/decisions.md`.
- Verification:
  - `test_optimisation_algorithms.py`: 35 passed (the checks, and the options that each algorithm gets, with a fake optimizer).
  - `04_test_optimization.py` with EnergyPlus 9.6: each of the 15 algorithms runs with the settings it needs and `evaluations=5`, so that it iterates (OMOPSO, SMPSO, CMAES and IBEA with the ranges of the custom model), and ParticleSwarm stops with the error; 6 passed. Before the fix of EvolutionaryStrategy, it was the only one that failed (`unexpected number of offspring, expected 2, received 1`). IBEA failed once, randomly, with the three categories of `CAT` and a population of 2 ("objective with empty range": platypus normalises the objectives by their range, so it stops when all the solutions of a generation have the same objectives); documented in the docstring, and the test gives it numeric ranges (it then ran twice without errors).
- Next step: the author confirms the decision (it changes the results of 5 algorithms).

#### [done] accim 1.0.0: parametric tests that run, two fixes of the parametric module, and integer actuators of the API backend
- Context: After the push of the samples and notebooks, the author asked to commit, push and keep working. Pending: the numbered tests and the full-workflow scripts of the parametric module (which had never run), and the integer actuators of the API backend.
- Changes:
  - Numbered tests (`tests/parametric_and_optimisation/NN_test_*.py`, tests only): the output readers before `set_problem()` (new helper `set_meter_readers`), the range samplings with the custom model and tuples (`CUSTOM_RANGES`; the predefined model only takes options), booleans in `minimize_outputs`, the parameters of the aPMV setpoints in `accis_params_dict` with the VRF system and the PMV inputs of notebook 03, `estimate_optimisation_sims(epws=...)`, `_save_idf_backup(out_dir=...)`, the timestamped result files (`latest_output`), valid comfort models (ComfStand 0 needs ComfMod 0, see `TODO.md`), two EPWs in the multi-EPW tests (they had one), and assertions instead of logs. `tests/test_setup.py`: `enable_sizing()` (the IDFs `SF_Detached_*` have zone sizing off and no sizing periods, which the VRF systems need), and a run period of two weeks across June and July with 2 timesteps per hour (two months of hourly results; about 11 s per simulation instead of 33).
  - Full-workflow scripts (`tests/parametric_and_optimisation_full_workflow`): `enable_sizing()`, valid comfort models, part B of `test_02` with `SF_Detached_D` (as its docstring says; it used an OSM model of EnergyPlus 25.2, but eppy reads all the models of a process with the IDD of the first one, of 9.6), `accis.add_accis_to_idf`, the meters of the wrapper without the test simulation (its baseline is not a valid comfort model, see `TODO.md`), the version of SALib with `importlib.metadata`, and `test_04` no longer replaces `problem.names` (which left the plots without parameters). README: run them with pytest from their folder (run directly on Windows, the processes of the simulations import the script again and break the pool).
  - `accim.parametric_and_optimisation` (internal fixes found by these tests; the usage does not change; the same errors in 0.8.2): the log of the evaluations of `run_optimisation()` had the path of the EPW in its name (`OSError: [Errno 22]` on Windows with EPWs given as paths), and `run_sensitivity_analysis_by_epw(method='sobol')` stopped with `KeyError: 'names'` (SALib's Sobol results have no names). New default tests for both.
  - Python API backend: the controller finds the type of each actuator when it first finds it (it writes 0.5, reads it back, restores the value and releases the actuator), and sets the integer actuators to the number rounded down and the logical ones to true only for 1, as the EMS (`EMSManager.cc`); the API rounds the integers (`std::lround`) and takes the numbers within 1e-5 of 1 as true (`datatransfer.cc`, the same in 24.2, 25.1 and 26.2). New Erl functions `@WarnEP`, `@SevereWarnEP` and `@FatalHaltEP` (messages in the error file through `issue_warning`/`issue_severe` of the API; the programs that use them run with the exact rules, so that a message is never reported twice).

#### [done] accim 1.0.0: refactoring to the conventions of Python and Python API backend (branch `v1.0.0`)
- Context: The author asked (in Spanish) for a refactoring of accim to the standards of Python, more efficient if possible, free to split the code into new files, with the possibility of using accim through the Python API of EnergyPlus in parallel to the current approach (eppy and EMS), keeping the usage similar (the names of classes and arguments may change where they break rules), in a new branch `v1.0.0`, working autonomously. Later, the author asked not to change the usage of `accim.parametric_and_optimisation`.
- Changes:
  - `accim.sim` rebuilt in small modules: `accis` (public API: `AddAccis`, `add_accis_to_idf`, `modify_accis`, `gen_outputs_df`, `load_idf`), `arguments` (valid values, checks, `LEGACY_ARGUMENT_NAMES`), `prompts`, `model` (`AccimModel`: occupied zones, windows, thermostats and existing HVAC), `targets`, `people`, `build` (the sequence that adds the ACCIS, shared by files and IDF objects), `outputs`, `generation` (combinations, names and `IdfWriter`, which renders the static objects once and only the 5 programs that change for each output IDF, with the bytes of eppy's `save`), `standards`, `idf_tools`, `ems/` (`program_lines`, `setpoint_lines`, `setast`, `programs`, `objects`, `erl`) and `hvac/` (`vrf`, `vrf_data`, `existing`, `resolver`). The old internal modules (`accim_*`, `setAST_models`, `dicts`, `lists`, `utils`) were removed.
  - PEP 8 names with deprecated aliases (`accim/_deprecation.py`): `addAccis` (subclass of `AddAccis`), `accis_single_idf`, `accis_single_idf_funcs`, `apmv_setpoints` (shims), `runEp`, `removefiles`, `genCSVconcatenated`, `preview_Table_cols`, and the old keyword arguments (renamed with a `DeprecationWarning`; both names at once raise `TypeError`).
  - Python API backend: `accim/sim/ems/erl.py` compiles the Erl programs to Python functions (fast path on floats, and a fallback evaluator with the exact rules of `RuntimeLanguageProcessor.cc` for Null, uninitialised values and errors); `accim/sim/energyplus_api/` (`idf`: the IDF that the API runs, without programs, calling managers and actuators, with global variables for the reported variables; `controller`: `ErlController`, which does at each calling point what `EMSManager.cc` does; `runner`: `run_simulation`, `run_simulations`; `runtime`: loads `pyenergyplus` from the installation, EnergyPlus 24.2 or later); `accim.run.run_ep(backend='ems'|'api')`.
  - `accim.utils` split into a package whose modules are imported lazily (`people`, `editing`, `accim_args`, `versions`, `outputs`, `verification`, `workspace`), so `accim.sim` does not import besos. New `accim.constants` and `accim.energyplus` (`find_energyplus`, `idd_path`, `read_idf_version`, `ACCIM_ENERGYPLUS_DIR`). `accim/__init__.py` has no side effects; the patches of besos, dask and platypus are in `accim/_besos_compat.py`, applied by `accim.parametric_and_optimisation`.
  - `accim.parametric_and_optimisation`: only its internal calls changed (`accis.add_accis_to_idf`, `accis.modify_accis`, `accim.sim.apmv`); its classes and arguments are the same.
  - `accim.data`: `gen_csv_concatenated` and `preview_table_cols` (with aliases), `name_export_rows_with_nan` in `Table` (the old name is accepted), unused imports (`datapackage`, `numpy`, `csv`...) removed.
  - Fixes found by the comparison with 0.8.2 (listed in `CHANGELOG.md`): combinations without setpoints and natural ventilation with the controls 2 and 3 skipped, the tolerances of `np.arange`, `ThermostatSetpoint:SingleCooling`, `make_averages`, calling managers of the programs of the input model, repeated outputs, `modify_accis` with `None` and without `SetVOFinputData`, the prompts that replaced `name_suffix`, `output_idfs`, People objects created again, and the order of the zones.
  - Documentation: migration guide (`docs/source/9_migration_guide.md`), section 4.9 (Python API backend) and new names in `1_requirements`, `2_installation`, `3_quick tutorial`, `4_detailed use`, `5_troubleshooting`, `index.rst` and `README.md`; API reference regenerated with `sphinx-apidoc`.
  - `setup.py` excludes the `tests` package (pending since 0.8.2).
- Files: `accim/__init__.py`, `accim/_besos_compat.py`, `accim/_deprecation.py`, `accim/constants.py`, `accim/energyplus.py`, `accim/lists.py`, `accim/sim/**`, `accim/utils/**`, `accim/run/**`, `accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`, `accim/data/postprocessing/utils.py`, `accim/data/preprocessing.py`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/__init__.py`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `tests/core/**`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/10_test_outputs_preflight.py`, `docs/source/**`, `README.md`, `setup.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `notes/work-log.md`, `notes/decisions.md`.
- Verification:
  - Golden master (session tooling, outside the repository): 29 configurations (`AddAccis` with all the script types, comfort standards, PMV, OpenStudio and DesignBuilder models, EnergyPlus 25.1 to 26.2, `People:Instance`, `output_take_dataframe`, tolerance ranges; `add_accis_to_idf`, `modify_accis`, `gen_outputs_df`, the deprecated class, and the aPMV setpoints) generated with 0.8.2 and with the new code: all the output IDFs are byte-identical, once the corrections above are applied to the files of 0.8.2 (checked one by one) and the IDD defaults that 0.8.2 added to the People objects are normalised. Run again with the final code.
  - EMS against API (EnergyPlus 25.1, 25.2 and 26.2): the CSV files of both backends are identical for the 10 model types of accim (also over a whole year: 52,560 timesteps, in the same time) and for 9 of the 11 EMS examples of EnergyPlus; `EMSPlantLoopOverrideControl` differs by 3e-9 °C in 3 columns, and `EMSPlantOperation_largeOff` stops with the error for the calling point `UserDefinedComponentModel`.
  - `pytest tests/core`: 279 passed (130 on `master`), including the simulations with EnergyPlus 26.2 and a new test that runs an output IDF with both backends. New test files: `test_erl.py`, `test_generation.py`, `test_energyplus_api.py`, `test_deprecation.py`, `test_utils_package.py`. `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 209 passed, as on `master`. The numbered files `01`, `08` and `10` (not collected by default): 39 passed and 2 failed (`test_idf_backup` without `out_dir`, and `test_accim_predef_models_parametric` without outputs), which fail in the same way on `master`.
  - Speed (sample `TestModel`, `vrf_mm`, 140 output IDFs): `AddAccis` 5.8-7.2 s instead of 19.4-20.8 s, and `from accim.sim import accis` 1.3-1.7 s instead of 15.9-24.4 s.
  - pyflakes (temporary installation): no undefined names in `accim`. Local Sphinx build: no warnings from the pages (only the docstrings of 0.8.2). `python -m build --wheel`: 77 files, without `tests` or `sample_files`.
- Review: a code review of the branch (by a review agent) found 6 problems, all fixed and covered by tests: (1) in the API backend, an operator whose operands are not both numbers (for instance, with Null) gave Null, but `EvaluateExpression` of EnergyPlus starts with the number 0 and only changes it when the operands are numbers, so it gives an unset 0 (checked in `RuntimeLanguageProcessor.cc` of 24.2, 25.1, 26.1 and 26.2): new `UNINITIALISED_NUMBER`, actuated with 0 instead of released; (2) an EMS output variable that names a program but reports a global variable (as `FindEMSVariable` allows) reported 0; (3) sums of more than 200 terms (the building averages of `make_averages` in models with more than 200 zones) did not compile in Python: the chains of one operator are written without nested parentheses, and the programs that Python still cannot compile are evaluated with the exact rules; (4) `0 ^ -1` stopped the simulation, while `std::pow` gives an infinity; (5) the EMS backend of `run_ep` cut the name of the EPW at its first dot (behaviour of 0.8.2, which made EPWs such as `Seville.2050.epw` and `Seville.2080.epw` overwrite each other's results): both backends now use `simulation_name()`; (6) the deprecated class `accis_single_idf.addAccis` did not keep the positional arguments and the `arguments` of 0.x, and `AddAccis.arguments.get()` and `in` did not accept the old names. After the fixes, the golden master and the comparison of both backends were run again with the same results.
- Next step: the sample scripts and the notebooks still use the names of 0.x (they work, with warnings): update them and run the notebooks again; then merge and release 1.0.0 (with accim-samples 1.0.0).

#### [done] Zone-level energy, EMS columns and names of the zones in `Table`
- Context: The other two tasks of `Table` in `TODO.md`: the zone-level energy units and EMS columns of the DesignBuilder models (added that morning), which concern the other models too (entry below), and the zones whose names contain the name of another zone (found in the work of the entry below). They change columns that users may rely on, so the author decided the design first: divide all the energy of each zone by the floor area of the zone, name the EMS outputs of the zones after their zones in all the models, as `Table` already did in the OpenStudio models, and find the columns of each zone by its whole name. Built on the work of the entry below (same branch, not committed yet).
- Changes (`accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`):
  - `_longest_name_in()` (new): the longest of the names that a text contains, which is the zone of a column (`Block1:Zone10`, not `Block1:Zone1`, in the columns of `Block1:Zone10`).
  - `_replace_names()` (new): replaces names of zones in the columns in one pass, with a regular expression that tries the longest names first, so that the name of a zone is not replaced within the name of another, nor twice. `Table` uses it to rename the zones after their spaces and to add their blocks, which it did name by name.
  - `_occupied_zones()`: the EMS suffix of each zone becomes the name of the zone in `Table` (`Block1_Zone1` becomes `BLOCK1:ZONE1`, `Zone_1` becomes `ZONE 1`, and `Space_2_People_Block1_Zone2`, in a zone with one space not named after it, becomes `BLOCK1:ZONE2`), except in the zones named after their spaces, whose suffix keeps the name of the People object (entry below). So `Table` renames these columns after their zones, as it did in the OpenStudio models (`Block1:Zone1_Comfortable Hours_No Applicability (h)`, `Block1:Zone1_Zone Floor Area (m2)`), and the EMS outputs that it does not rename have the name of the zone (`EMS:OCCUPIED HOURS_BLOCK1:ZONE1 [H](HOURLY)`).
  - `Table.__init__`: the zone of each column (`zone_of`) is the longest of the names of the zones that it contains. The occupied zones of the models without SpaceLists, the zone totals, the block totals (the columns of the zones of each block, and the columns of no zone by the longest name of a block they contain), the excluded zones of `level_excluded_zones` and the renaming of the zone columns use it. With `normalised_energy_units=True`, the energy of each zone (cooling and heating demand and consumption, and the zone totals) is divided by the floor area of the zone, its EMS output; `Table` prints the energy columns whose zone or floor area it does not find, which are not divided. `hvac_zone_list_underscore` is no longer used. The docstring of `normalised_energy_units` says what is divided by what.
- Files: `accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`, `tests/core/test_table_columns.py`, `tests/core/test_table_spaces.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification:
  - `tests/core/test_table_columns.py`: 5 new tests (6 cases: the zone-level energy of a DesignBuilder model divided by the floor area of each zone, its EMS outputs named after their zones, zones without spaces named `Zone 1` and `Zone 2`, the zones `Block1:Zone1` and `Block1:Zone10`, the blocks `Block1` and `Block10`, and an excluded zone whose name is part of another), and the test of the normalised energy of the OpenStudio models now checks the energy of the zones too (2 cases). The 8 cases fail with the code of the entry below. In `tests/core/test_table_spaces.py`, two tests checked the raw names of the EMS outputs (a model without spaces, and a SpaceList without Space Types); they now check their new names.
  - `pytest tests/core`: 127 passed (121 before). `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 209 passed, as before.

    - the EMS columns of the zones renamed after their zones, with the same values: 20 with `vrf_ac` and 22 with `vrf_mm` in NS, Z2S and SL, and 18 in the 12 CSV files of the sample notebook of `Table`; none in OSL, SL_os and OSM, which already had them;
    - the energy columns of the zones divided by the floor area of their zone in the 8760 hours: 12 in each model (the cooling and heating demand and consumption, and the zone totals, of two zones), and 4 in OSM, whose zone totals were already divided and which has no heating.
  - The block and building totals, the other columns and the lists of zones do not change, and OSM without normalisation is identical. In all the cases, the energy of the zones weighted by their floor areas equals the building totals (differences below 1e-13 kWh/m2). With fake models, the zones `Block1:Zone1` and `Block1:Zone10` give the same columns and the right zone totals with `PYTHONHASHSEED` from 0 to 5. Before, the zone total of `Block1:Zone1` added up both zones, and with 5 both zones got the same column names. The zones `Zone 1` and `Zone 10` no longer stop with normalisation.

#### [done] Zone and block totals of `Table` with OpenStudio models
- Context: Task of `TODO.md` (P2) added the same day by the work on `Table` with zones of several occupant instances (entry below), on whose commit this work is built (`7697c1b`, branch `claude/keen-torvalds-5a3ddf`, not yet merged into `master`). The other task added that day (zone-level energy units and EMS columns of `Table` with DesignBuilder models) waits for the decision of the author, because it changes columns that users may rely on.
- Checked first, with the annual simulations of the entry below (EnergyPlus 25.1, Seville, `vrf_ac` and `vrf_mm`; `Table` at block and building level, sum and mean, for the run period and hourly) and with fake models (scripts and results outside the repository). With the code of `7697c1b`:
  - OSM (`OSM_TestResidentialUnit_v01_onlygeometry`: zones `Floor_1_Zone` and `Floor_2_Zone`, spaces `Floor_1` and `Floor_2`): with `normalised_energy_units=True`, `Table` stopped with `IndexError: list index out of range`, because the normalisation looked for the floor area of each zone with the name of the zone (`Floor_1_Zone`), and the EMS floor area outputs end with `<Space>_<People>` (`EMS:ZONE FLOOR AREA_RESIDENTIAL:FLOOR_1_RESIDENTIAL_LIVING_OCCUPANTS`, once `Table` adds the block). Without normalisation, the zone totals (`Floor_1_Zone_Total Energy Demand (kWh) (summed)` and `Total Energy Consumption`) were 0, because `Table` looked for the columns of each zone with the name of the zone after renaming the zones after their spaces (`RESIDENTIAL:FLOOR_1 VRF INDOOR UNIT ...`).
  - OSL (the sample TestModel with one space per zone, `Space 1` and `Space 2`, and the Space Types of OpenStudio): zone totals of 0 too. The EMS outputs end with `Space_1_Occupants`, with the blank replaced by `_`, so they did not get the block when `Table` renamed `SPACE 1` to `BLOCK1_SPACE 1`: they kept their raw names, the block totals of comfort hours, floor area and air volume were 0 (`nan` with the mean), and those of energy, divided by that floor area, `inf`.
  - SL_os: the zone named after its space (Block1:Zone2, `Space 2`) had zone totals of 0 too.
  - Found too, and not changed (added to the other task of `TODO.md`): in the models without spaces whose zones are not named `block:zone` (fake models), the zones `Zone 1` and `Zone 2` have the same problems as OSL (EMS outputs without the block, block totals of comfort hours of 0) and `Table` stops with `IndexError` with normalisation; with `ZoneOne` and `ZoneTwo`, the zone totals are divided by the floor area, but not the other zone-level energy columns. No model has the zone-level energy columns (demand and consumption of cooling and heating) divided by the floor area, although they are labelled kWh/m2. And with the zones `Block1:Zone1` and `Block1:Zone10` (fake model), the zone total of `Block1:Zone1` also adds up the demand of `Block1:Zone10`, and with some orders of `occupied_zone_list`, which comes from `list(set(...))`, the columns of both zones get the same names (new task in `TODO.md`).
- Changes (`accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`):
  - `_occupied_zones()`: the EMS suffix of a zone named after its space, when the name of the space has characters that EMS names cannot have (`Space_1_Occupants`, for `Space 1`), gets back the name of the space (`SPACE 1_OCCUPANTS`), so the EMS outputs of the zone get the block and are renamed after the zone, as those of the spaces without such characters already were (OSM).
  - `Table.__init__`: the name of each occupied zone in the columns (`zone_names_in_columns`: after its space, with its block); the zone totals take the columns of the HVAC system of each zone with that name, and the normalisation looks for the floor area of each zone with it. The names of the zone totals do not change (those of `hvac_zone_list`).
- Files: `accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`, `tests/core/test_table_columns.py` (new), `tests/core/test_table_spaces.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification:
  - `tests/core/test_table_columns.py` (new, 6 tests: zone totals, block totals and normalised energy units of an OpenStudio model with the names of OSM, and of one with the default names of OpenStudio, `Thermal Zone 1` and `Space 1`): the 6 fail with the code of `7697c1b`. In `tests/core/test_table_spaces.py`, the test of the OpenStudio model with `Space 1` and `Space 2` checked that the EMS outputs kept their raw names; it now checks their new names and a block total.

In OSL, SL_os (both hierarchies) and OSM without normalisation, only the fixed columns change: the zone totals, which were 0, are equal to those of NS for OSL and to those of SL for SL_os (the same simulations), in the 8760 hours; the block totals of OSL, which were 0, `nan` or `inf`, are equal to those of NS; and the EMS columns of OSL are renamed after their zones (20 columns with `vrf_ac`, 22 with `vrf_mm`), with the same values. OSM with normalisation no longer stops: its zone totals are its zone totals in kWh divided by the floor area of each zone (46.45 m2), in the 8760 hours (97.1 and 99.8 kWh/m2 of annual demand with `vrf_ac`). The fake models without spaces (`Zone 1`, `ZoneOne`) and of DesignBuilder give the same columns and values as with `7697c1b`.
