#### [done] `Table` with zones of several occupant instances
- Context: Task of `TODO.md` (P2) added on 2026-10-18 by the work on the names of the occupant instances (entry below), on whose commit this work is built (`23d36b1`, branch `claude/jolly-antonelli-194839`, not yet merged into `master`). Since that commit, accim simulates models with `Space` objects where a zone has several occupant instances, whose EMS objects are named after the zone, while those of the zones with one instance keep `<Space>_<People>`; before, those models stopped in EnergyPlus, so `Table` had never read their results.
- Checked first (scripts and results outside the repository): annual simulations with EnergyPlus 25.1 in Seville (`runEp`) of the sample `TestModel` of 25.1 without `Space` objects (NS); with the floor of Block1:Zone1 split in two spaces, a space in Block1:Zone2 and the `People` objects assigned to the zones (Z2S); with those spaces in a SpaceList and one `People` object, `People Block1:Zone1`, assigned to it (SL; also read by `Table` with the Space Types of OpenStudio in the spaces, SL_os); with one space per zone, a SpaceList with the Space Types of OpenStudio and one `People` object `Occupants` (OSL, like the OpenStudio models that worked before); and the sample `OSM_TestResidentialUnit_v01_onlygeometry` (OSM). `addAccis` with `vrf_ac` (HVACmode 0) and `vrf_mm` (HVACmode 2), EN 16798-1, CAT 3, ComfMod 3; `Table` at block and building level (sum and mean), for the run period and hourly. With the code of `23d36b1`:
  - Z2S: each output of the occupants of Block1:Zone1 (the running mean outdoor temperatures of EN 16798-1 and ASHRAE 55; also PMV and PPD when they are reported) appeared twice with the same name, one per instance (`Space 1a People Block1:Zone1` and `Space 1b People Block1:Zone1`).
  - SL: no occupied zones, because `Table` looked for the spaces whose Space Type is the name of the SpaceList, so the zone columns kept their raw names and there were no block totals.
  - SL_os: Block1:Zone1 was named after `Space 1a`, and `Space 1b` was an occupied zone with only the outputs of its instance; with `block_zone_hierarchy` listing Block1:Zone1, the zone became `BLOCK1:BLOCK1:ZONE1`. The instance of Block1:Zone2 (`Space 2 People Block1:Zone1`) and its EMS suffix (`Space_2_People_Block1_Zone1`) contain the name of Block1:Zone1, and `Table` attributes the columns to the zone whose name they contain, so they can be taken for those of Block1:Zone1.
  - The building totals were right in all cases, and so were NS and OSL. The hourly readers of the parametric module do not depend on the names of the zones: `compute_hourly_indicators` gives the same indicators for Z2S and SL, and for NS and OSL (the split floor changes them slightly: 26.295 °C against 26.308 °C of mean operative temperature with `vrf_ac`).
  - Found too, and not changed here (they were there before; two new tasks in `TODO.md`): in the models whose zones are named after their spaces (OSL, OSM), the zone columns `Total Energy Demand` and `Total Energy Consumption` (summed) are 0, and the block totals of OSL are divided by a block floor area of 0 (`inf`); OSM stops with `IndexError` in the normalisation by floor area (`normalised_energy_units=True`, the default); in the models without spaces, the zone-level energy is labelled kWh/m2 but is not divided by the floor area, and the EMS outputs of the zones keep their raw names. The output discovery of the parametric module gives the EMS outputs of a zone with several instances the object `EMS` instead of the zone (their names have no space name); that column is informative only.
- Changes (`accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`):
  - `_occupied_zones()`: the occupied zones from the targets of the EMS of accim (`_resolve_targets`, one per occupied zone, imported in the function as in `scan_zones`; importing `accim.sim.apmv_setpoints` applies its patch of `besos.objectives.read_eso`, as `addAccis` already did), and the names `Table` gives them: in the models with SpaceLists, the name of the space for a zone with one instance placed through a SpaceList named like the Space Type of its space (as before), and the name of the zone otherwise. It also gives the columns to rename after their zone: the outputs of the instances of a zone with several instances, and, in models with `Space` objects, the outputs of a zone with one instance whose names contain the name of another zone (the outputs keyed by the instance, and the EMS outputs and schedules that end with its suffix, which becomes the sanitized zone name, as for a zone with several instances, or the name of the space if the zone is named after it).
  - `_rename_and_merge_columns()`: the columns that get the same name are merged, with their mean for the outputs that `Table` averages over time (`_AVERAGED_OUTPUTS`, the list of the aggregation over time, moved to the module) and their sum for the others.
  - `Table.__init__` uses them before the occupied zones are found. The branch of the models with SpaceLists renames the zones after their spaces in the same order as before (People objects, then Space objects). When `Table` asks for the blocks, the zones whose names already have the pattern `block:zone` keep them, so `block_zone_hierarchy` only needs the others; the parameter is now documented. The import of `get_people_objects` in `Table.__init__` is no longer needed.
- Files: `accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`, `tests/core/test_table_spaces.py` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification:
  - `tests/core/test_table_spaces.py` (new, 7 tests, with an eppy-like model and CSV files with the names of the columns of the simulations above and made-up values): the zone of two spaces, two `People` objects in a zone without spaces, the SpaceList without Space Types and with them (two hierarchies), and two models that worked before (OpenStudio with one space per zone, and a model without spaces). The first five fail with the code of `23d36b1` (duplicated columns, a missing zone column, no occupied zones, `Space 1b` as an occupied zone, `BLOCK1:BLOCK1:ZONE1`); the other two pass with both.
  - `pytest tests/core`: 115 passed (108 before), in 4 minutes. `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 209 passed, as before.
  - With the simulations above (`PYTHONHASHSEED=0`): the DataFrames and the lists of zones of NS and OSL (run period and hourly, `vrf_ac` and `vrf_mm`), of OSM without normalisation, and of the 12 CSV files of the sample notebook of `Table` are identical to those of `23d36b1`, and OSM with normalisation stops with the same `IndexError`. Z2S, SL and SL_os have no duplicated columns and find the zones Block1:Zone1 and Block1:Zone2 (SL_os, `BLOCK1:ZONE1` and `BLOCK1:SPACE 2` with `block_zone_hierarchy={'Block1': ['Space 2']}`); in the 8760 hours, their hourly zone columns (operative temperature, running mean outdoor temperature as the mean of the instances, cooling demand and comfortable hours) are equal to the columns of each zone in the CSV files, and SL has the same columns as NS.
- Next step: the two new tasks of `TODO.md`. This branch is built on `23d36b1`, which is not merged with `master` (`81903b8`).

### 2026-10-18
#### [done] Names of the occupant instances in models with Space objects
- Context: Task of `TODO.md` (P1) added the same day by the work on `People:Definition` and `People:Instance` (entry below), which found that in models with `Space` objects `_resolve_targets` gave the EMS sensors keys that EnergyPlus does not create. Built on that work (`91b5bfd`, not yet merged into `master`), with the permission of the author, who decided before the implementation: detect the remainder spaces as EnergyPlus does, with a warning; make a zone with several occupant instances one target, named after the zone, with the occupancy of the zone and the PMV of its instances weighted by their occupants; and keep the names of the targets of the zones with one instance (only the sensor keys that were wrong change).
- Checked first with EnergyPlus 25.2 and 26.2 (1-day simulations of 20 variants of the sample `TestModel`, with the names read from the ESO and from the `People Internal Gains Nominal` lines of the eio; both versions give the same names, and both stop with the variant that adds a second `Space` without surfaces to a zone): assigned to a space, the instance is `<People>`, also in a zone of several spaces; assigned to a zone, `<People>` if the zone has one space and `<Space> <People>` for each space if it has several; assigned to a space list or a zone list, `<Space> <People>` for each space, also with one member. The default space of a zone without `Space` objects, also in models that have them, has the name of the zone. EnergyPlus creates `<ZONE>-REMAINDER` only when some heat transfer surfaces of a zone with `Space` objects have a Space Name and others do not (`CreateMissingSpaces` in `SurfaceGeometry.cc`: InternalMass, subsurfaces and shading surfaces do not count; checked with walls without space, which create it, and with an InternalMass without space, which does not), and the only `Space` of a zone, if it has no surfaces, does not create it either. The names do not depend on the case. The old resolver gave the names of EnergyPlus in 4 of the 13 main variants: it failed with the occupants assigned to a space or to a zone of one space, left out the zones without `Space` objects of the models that have them, and ignored the remainder spaces. `Zone People Occupant Count` (key: the zone) adds up the occupants of all the instances of a zone in 25.2 and 26.2.
- Changes:
  - `accim.utils`: new `get_energyplus_spaces()` (the spaces of each zone as EnergyPlus creates them; the remainder space comes from the Space Name of the 14 classes of heat transfer surfaces that have one, `SURFACE_OBJECTS_WITH_SPACE_NAME`) and `get_people_instances()` (the instances of the `People` and `People:Instance` objects, with the names of EnergyPlus, their space, zone and assignment). `get_people_hierarchy()`, `get_people_names_for_ems()` and `get_idf_hierarchy_with_people()` use them: they include the default and remainder spaces, and `get_people_names_for_ems()` returns the names of EnergyPlus (before, `<Space> <People>` also for the occupants of a space, and nothing for the zones without `Space` objects). `identify_variable_key_pattern()` checks `<Zone> <People>` before `<Space> <People>`, for the default spaces.
  - `_resolve_targets`: one target per occupied zone. With one instance, the target is as before (`df_key` and `ems_suffix` are the zone name for occupants assigned to a zone without `Space` objects, and `<Space> <People>` otherwise), and its sensor key is the name of the instance. With several, the target is named after the zone, its sensor key is its first instance, and its occupancy is the `Zone People Occupant Count` of the zone; the remainder spaces give a warning. The targets have four new keys: `occupancy_key`, `occupancy_variable`, `instance_keys` and `people_names`.
  - `addAccis`: `scan_zones` gives `ems_occupancy_keys` and `ems_occupancy_variables`, which `addEMSSensorsBase` uses for `Occ_count_<target>` and `<window>_Occ_count`. With one target per zone, `addForscriptSchVRFsystem` no longer creates the `ThermostatSetpoint:DualSetpoint` `<Zone> Dual SP` once per instance (`Duplicate name found` in EnergyPlus), and `ex_ac` no longer creates the actuators of the existing thermostats for the first target of each zone only (`Actuator ... = AHST_ACT_SPACE_1B_PEOPLE_BLOCK1_ZONE1 not found`).
  - aPMV setpoints: in a zone with several instances, a PMV sensor and an occupancy sensor per instance (`PMV_<zone>_<n>`, `People_Occupant_Count_<zone>_<n>`), the global variable `PMV_<zone>` and the program `average_PMV_<zone>`, called before `monitor_aPMV_<zone>`, with the average of the PMV of the instances weighted by their occupants (the simple average while the zone is unoccupied), as the PeopleAverage method of the Fanger thermostats.
  - `set_pmv_input_parameters`: each `People` object takes the value of the first target of its instances, with a warning if its targets have different values. Before, the occupants assigned to a zone of one or several spaces matched no key and did not change.
  - Parametric module: the output discovery maps the keys of the outputs of the occupants to their spaces with `get_people_instances()`.
  - Requirements and Detailed use (4.8): the occupants of the models with `Space` objects, and the names of the zones in the dictionaries of the aPMV setpoints. `tests/README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. In `TODO.md`, the P1 task is closed and there are two new P2 tasks (see Next step).
- Files: `accim/utils.py`, `accim/sim/apmv_setpoints.py`, `accim/sim/utils.py`, `accim/sim/accim_Base_EMS.py`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `tests/core/test_space_instance_names.py` (new), `tests/core/test_energyplus_versions.py`, `tests/core/test_people_instance.py`, `tests/README.md`, `docs/source/1_requirements.md`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification:
  - `tests/core/test_space_instance_names.py` (new, 27 tests with eppy-like objects, without EnergyPlus): the names of 15 assignments (one with a `People:Instance` object), the remainder spaces, the targets, the hierarchy functions, `set_pmv_input_parameters`, `scan_zones`, the occupancy sensors of `addAccis` and the EMS of the aPMV setpoints. All fail with the code of `91b5bfd` (most of them on behaviour; the rest because the new functions do not exist there). In `test_energyplus_versions.py`, a new test with EnergyPlus 26.2, skipped without it: the sample model with the occupants assigned to a space and to a zone of one space, to a zone of two spaces and to a space list, with `addAccis` (`vrf_ac`) and with aPMV setpoints in the zone of two spaces, simulated for 1 day; with the code of `91b5bfd` it stops with `Invalid Output:Variable or Output:Meter Index Key Name = SPACE 2 PEOPLE BLOCK1:ZONE2`. In `test_people_instance.py`, the test that compares whole targets includes the new keys.
  - `pytest tests/core`: 108 passed (80 before), in 3 minutes. `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 209 passed, as before.
  - Campaign (scripts outside the repository), 1 day, Seville: the 18 variants that EnergyPlus can simulate (a 19th, with a space of one wall and no floor, stops in EnergyPlus with or without accim), with `addAccis` (`vrf_ac`, EN 16798-1 CAT 3, HVACmode 0) and with aPMV setpoints (`add_vrf_system`, `apply_apmv_setpoints`), and 8 of them with `vrf_mm` (HVACmode 2) and `ex_ac`: the 104 simulations with EnergyPlus 25.2 and 26.2 complete, with the same outcome in both versions. The severe errors are the known ones (warmup convergence and the orphaned `ZoneHVAC:IdealLoadsAirSystem` objects of the sample model) and, in the variants with a remainder space without floor, `ComputeIntSolarAbsorpFactors`. With the code of `80a33f9` (the same resolver as `91b5bfd` for `People` objects), 12 of the 54 simulations with 26.2 complete: the models without `Space` objects, the space list with one space per zone, and a zone list where the old resolver left out the zone without `Space` objects and the remainder space.
  - Occupancy and PMV of a zone with two `People` objects of different PMV and number of occupants (2 people at 200 W, and People/Area), 1 day with EnergyPlus 26.2: the zone PMV of the EMS is the weighted average of the PMV of the instances in the 144 timesteps, and the occupancy of the zone, their sum (differences of 0).
  - Regression: the output IDFs of `addAccis` (`vrf_ac`, `vrf_mm`, `ex_ac`) and of the aPMV setpoints for the sample models that work today (the OpenStudio residential unit of 25.1 and 25.2, the OpenStudio small office converted to 25.1, `TestModel` of 9.4, 9.6 and 25.1, `SF_Detached_B_min_North` of 9.6 and the small office of the case study of 25.1) are identical byte by byte to those of `91b5bfd` (16 cases, with `PYTHONHASHSEED=0`: `scan_zones` orders the zones with a `set`), and `_resolve_targets` gives the same targets as `91b5bfd` for the 29 sample IDFs of the repository (EnergyPlus 9.4 to 25.2).
  - After merging `master` (`75e1f68`, with the fixes of `TempCtrl='pmv'` and of `ComfStand=[0]` as the only standard) on 2026-10-19: `pytest tests/core`, 111 passed; `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`, 209 passed. With EnergyPlus 26.2, `addAccis` with `TempCtrl='pmv'` and with `ComfStand=[0]` (`vrf_ac`) on 8 of the variants (one space per zone with the occupants in the zones and in the spaces, a zone of two spaces, space lists, a remainder space, a zone list with a zone without `Space` objects, no `Space` objects, and two `People` objects in a zone): the 16 simulations of 1 day complete, with the known severe errors.
- Next step: not changed here (see `TODO.md`): `Table` with models whose zones have several spaces, and the order of the zones of `scan_zones`, which changes from one process to another.

#### [done] `addAccis` with `ComfStand=[0]` as the only standard
- Context: Found while fixing `TempCtrl='pmv'` (see the entry below), and the author asked to fix it in the same branch. With `TempCtrl='temperature'` and `ComfStand=[0]` (Spanish CTE) as the only standard, `genIDF` stopped with `UnboundLocalError: cannot access local variable 'ComfMod_value'` (line 1389 with `vrf_ac`, line 1436 with `vrf_mm`). The branch of ComfStand 0 has no ComfMod loop, and since the SetAST refactoring (`95b695b`) it appends the lines of the comfort model with `ComfMod_value`. With another standard in the list, `ComfMod_value` kept the last value of the loops of that standard, which gives the same lines. accim 0.8.0 and 0.8.1 have it.
- Changes: the branch of ComfStand 0 of `genIDF` sets `ComfMod_value = 0`, next to the `set ComfMod = 0` it writes in `SetInputData`, with a comment. `get_SetAST_lines(0, ComfMod)` returns the same 37 lines for any ComfMod, so the output IDFs are the same as when another standard is in the list.
- Files: `accim/sim/accim_IDFgeneration.py`, `tests/core/test_addaccis_setast.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification:
  - `test_each_output_idf_has_the_setast_lines_of_its_comfort_model` (`tests/core/test_addaccis_setast.py`) has two new cases, `vrf_ac` and `vrf_mm` with `ComfStand=[0]`: the output IDF has the 4 base lines of `SetAST` followed by the lines of ComfStand 0. Both fail with the previous code (`UnboundLocalError` in lines 1389 and 1436). `pytest tests/core`: 69 passed (67 before). `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 208 passed, as before.
  - Simulations with EnergyPlus 26.2 (scripts kept outside the repository; the sample `TestModel` updated to 26.2 and the same two weeks as in the entry below): the output IDFs of `ComfStand=[0]` with `vrf_ac` (HVACmode 0) and `vrf_mm` (HVACmode 2, VentCtrl 0) complete, with the same messages as the same runs with ComfStand 1 (CAT 3, ComfMod 3), except the 2 severe errors of warmup convergence of the `vrf_ac` run with ComfStand 1. #### [done] People:Definition and People:Instance objects of EnergyPlus 26.2
- Context: Task of `TODO.md` of 2026-10-18 (P2). EnergyPlus 26.2 can describe the occupants with a `People:Instance` object (name, zone, zone list, space or space list, schedules of occupancy, activity, work efficiency, clothing insulation and air velocity, and a multiplier) that references a `People:Definition` object (number of people, heat gain fractions, CO2 rate and thermal comfort models 1 to 7), instead of a `People` object. accim only read `People` objects, and since the entry below `scan_zones` stopped with a `ValueError` for the models with the new objects. The author approved the design before the implementation.
- Checked first with EnergyPlus 26.2 (1-day simulations of the sample `TestModel` updated to 26.2, with the occupants assigned to a zone, a zone list, a space, a space list, a zone list of zones with spaces, a zone of one space and a zone of two spaces, each with `People` and with `People:Instance` objects): EnergyPlus names the instances of a `People:Instance` after the `People:Instance` object, never after its `People:Definition`, with the same rules as for `People` objects (Input Output Reference, 1.14.1): `<object>` for a space or a zone of one space, and `<space> <object>` for space lists, zone lists and zones of several spaces (the default space of a zone without `Space` objects has the name of the zone, and `<zone>-Remainder` gets the surfaces of a zone with spaces that have no space). The outputs that the EMS sensors of accim read (`People Occupant Count`, `Zone Thermal Comfort Fanger Model PMV` and the running mean outdoor temperatures of the ASHRAE 55 and CEN 15251 adaptive models) have those names when the thermal comfort models are in the `People:Definition`.
- Changes:
  - `accim.utils`: new `get_people_objects()` (the `People` objects and then the `People:Instance` objects; only the `People` objects with the IDDs before 26.2), `is_people_instance()` and `get_people_definition()`.
  - `_resolve_targets` reads both kinds of objects, with the same naming logic as before, so `scan_zones` (occupied zones, `ems_objs_key`) and the EMS sensors of `addAccis` (RMOT, PMOT, `Occ_count_<zone>`, `<window>_Occ_count`) and of `apply_apmv_setpoints` use the names of the instances; `accim_Base_EMS.py` does not change.
  - `scan_zones` accepts the models with `People:Instance` objects, and stops with a `ValueError` when the model has neither `People` nor `People:Instance` objects.
  - `setComfFieldsPeople`: the `People` objects as before. The `People:Definition` objects referenced by the `People:Instance` objects get, in place, AdaptiveASH55 and AdaptiveCEN15251 as models 1 and 2, Fanger as model 3 with `TempCtrl='pmv'` (else empty), and models 4 to 7 empty, as the `People` objects it creates again; a `People:Definition` that no instance references does not change, and a missing one stops with a `ValueError`. Its final `del ppl, firstpeopleobject` stopped with `UnboundLocalError` without `People` objects.
  - aPMV: `set_pmv_input_parameters` sets the schedules in the `People:Instance` objects and the Fanger model in their `People:Definition`; `set_zones_always_occupied` also changes the `People:Instance` objects.
  - The other readers of `People` objects: `get_people_hierarchy` (and so `get_people_names_for_ems`), `get_idf_hierarchy_with_people`, `set_occupancy_to_always`, `apply_heating_activation_time_sch` (`chile_funcs`), the occupied spaces of `Table` and `AnalysisMixin._resolve_occupied_zone_names` (floor area with `mode='occupied'` in the parametric module, which was 0 for these models).
  - Requirements (the limitation is gone), Detailed use (the comfort models and the PMV inputs of the `People:Instance` objects), `tests/README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Files: `accim/utils.py`, `accim/sim/apmv_setpoints.py`, `accim/sim/utils.py`, `accim/sim/accim_Base.py`, `accim/sim/chile_funcs.py`, `accim/data/postprocessing/main.py`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/analysis.py`, `tests/core/test_people_instance.py` (new), `tests/core/test_energyplus_versions.py`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_analysis_floor_area.py`, `tests/README.md`, `docs/source/1_requirements.md`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification:
  - `tests/core/test_people_instance.py` (new, 13 tests with eppy-like objects, without EnergyPlus): `get_people_objects`, `get_people_definition`, `_resolve_targets` with zones and with a zone list, `scan_zones`, `setComfFieldsPeople`, `set_pmv_input_parameters`, the always-occupied functions and the hierarchy functions. With the code of the previous commit the module does not import; with only the three new functions of `accim.utils` added to that code, 10 tests fail (the other 3 test those functions). In `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_analysis_floor_area.py`, a new test of the occupied floor area of a model with `People:Instance` objects (0 with the previous code).

For each, `addAccis` with `vrf_ac` (EN 16798-1 CAT 3, and ASHRAE 55 CAT 80), `vrf_mm` (HVACmode 2, VentCtrl 0) and the custom model (`ComfStand=99`), and aPMV setpoints. The 25 simulations complete. The severe errors are the known ones (warmup convergence and orphaned `ZoneHVAC:IdealLoadsAirSystem` objects), the same with `People` objects.

The `People:Definition` objects get AdaptiveASH55, AdaptiveCEN15251 and Fanger as models 1 to 3. #### [done] `addAccis(TempCtrl='pmv')` generates the output IDFs again
- Context: Task of `TODO.md`, found while checking EnergyPlus 26.2 (see the entry below). With `TempCtrl='pmv'`, `genIDF` stopped with `UnboundLocalError: cannot access local variable 'ComfStand_value'` with any version of EnergyPlus, before saving any output IDF. The SetAST refactoring (`95b695b`, 2026-05-04) made the PMV branch append the lines of a comfort model with `ComfStand_value` and `ComfMod_value`, which only exist in the loops of the temperature branch, and `dd116ea` kept them in the call to `replace_SetAST_dynamic_lines`. accim 0.8.0 and 0.8.1 have it; in 0.7.7, the PMV branch did not change `SetAST`.
- Changes: the PMV branch of `genIDF` no longer calls `replace_SetAST_dynamic_lines`, so `SetAST` keeps its 4 base lines (`SetpointAcc`, `m`, `n` and `run SetAST_Master`); a comment says why. Reasons:
  - Each occupied zone of a PMV IDF has a `ZoneControl:Thermostat:ThermalComfort` with a `ThermostatSetpoint:ThermalComfort:Fanger:DualSetpoint` (PMV -0.5 and 0.5, control type 4 all year). According to the Input Output Reference of EnergyPlus 25.2, a thermal comfort control with a non-zero control type overrides the `ZoneControl:Thermostat` of the zone, whose setpoint schedules the EMS sets to `AHST` and `ACST`.
  - The PMV IDF has no comfort model (`[CS_PMV[CA_X[CM_X`): the PMV branch only writes `set HVACmode = 0` in `SetInputData`, and the `ComfStand`, `CAT` and `ComfMod` arguments are not used.
  - `run SetAST_Master` still sets `ACST` and `AHST` with the default comfort model of `SetInputData` (ComfStand 1, CAT 1, ComfMod 2). Only `ApplyAST_<zone>`, `SetASTnoTol` and the programs that count comfort hours read them, and they are set before they are read, which EnergyPlus 26.1 and later require. 0.7.7 did the same with the monolithic `SetAST`.
- Files: `accim/sim/accim_IDFgeneration.py`, `tests/core/test_addaccis_setast.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification:
  - New test `test_pmv_output_idf_has_only_the_base_lines_of_setast` in `tests/core/test_addaccis_setast.py`: `addAccis` with `TempCtrl='pmv'` on the sample `TestModel` (EnergyPlus 25.1) saves one output IDF, `TestModel[CS_PMV[CA_X[CM_X[HM_0[VC_X[VO_X[MT_X[MW_X[AT_X[NS_X.idf`, removes the `_pymod` IDF, and the output IDF has the 4 base lines of `SetAST` and the Fanger thermostats of the 2 zones. It fails with the previous code (`UnboundLocalError` in line 1677). `pytest tests/core`: 67 passed (66 before). `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 208 passed, as before.
  - Simulations (scripts kept outside the repository): the sample `TestModel` updated to 25.2 and 26.2 with `update_idf_version`; `addAccis` (`vrf_ac`, `Output_type='standard'`, hourly) with `TempCtrl='pmv'` and, as reference, with `TempCtrl='temperature'` (ComfStand 1, CAT 3, ComfMod 3); only the RunPeriod changed, to 1-7 January and 1-7 July (two environments); Seville. The 4 simulations complete. With both versions, the PMV IDF has 17 warnings and 2 severe errors (the orphaned `ZoneHVAC:IdealLoadsAirSystem` objects), and the temperature IDF the same ones plus 2 severe errors of warmup convergence in January. No message appears only in the PMV run, and EnergyPlus 26.2 finds no variable read before it is set. `ACST` and `AHST` have values in every hour of the PMV run.
  - A copy of the PMV IDF with setpoint outputs (26.2): the comfort control type is 4 and the PMV setpoints are -0.5 and 0.5 in every hour, and the thermostat setpoints come from the Fanger control, not from the schedules of `AHST` and `ACST` (zone 1, occupied hours: cooling setpoint 23.9 °C on average against an `ACST` of 27.2 °C).
- Next step: `genIDF` also stops with `UnboundLocalError` (`ComfMod_value`) when `ComfStand=[0]` is the only standard of a temperature run (new task in `TODO.md`; not changed here). Seen and not investigated: in the PMV runs, the hourly PMV of the occupied hours is above 0.55 in 80 and 65 of 120 hours (zones 1 and 2; up to 0.75 in January and 1.09 in July), with the zone air above the Fanger cooling setpoint while the VRF indoor units deliver 13-14 kW (autosized at 1.2 MW). This does not depend on `SetAST`.

#### [done] EnergyPlus 26.1 and 26.2
- Context: The author installed EnergyPlus 26.2 (`C:\EnergyPlusV26-2-0`) and asked to update accim to use it (task of `TODO.md` of 2026-10-17). EnergyPlus 26.1 is not installed, but the installation of 26.2 has the IDDs of 25.2, 26.1 and 26.2 and the transition programs: both versions were checked against the IDD, and only 26.2 was simulated. EnergyPlus 25.2 was used as reference.
- Changes:
  - `'26.1'` and `'26.2'` in `accim.lists` (`fullEPversionsList`, `epvers_space_objs`), `accim.sim.lists`, the list of `accis_single_idf_funcs.addAccis` (it rejected the IDFs of 26.x even with `EnergyPlus_version=None`, so the parametric module could not use them), `get_idd_path_from_ep_version` and the ReadVarsESO search of `read_eso_using_readvarseso` (26.2 and 26.1 first). The prompts, messages and docstrings say 9.1 to 26.2.
  - EMS: new program `InitASTvariables`, called at `BeginNewEnvironment` (`addEMSProgramsBase`, `addEMSPCMBase`), which sets `m`, `n`, `ACSTnoTol` and `AHSTnoTol` to 0. In each timestep, `SetComfTemp` reads `m` and `n` (custom model, `ComfStand=99`) and `CountHours_<zone>` reads `ACSTnoTol` and `AHSTnoTol` before `SetAST` and `SetASTnoTol` set them. Since EnergyPlus 26.1 (NatLabRockies/EnergyPlus#11409, followed in 26.2 by NatLabRockies/EnergyPlus#11551), the variables read before they are set during the setup of the simulation are reset before each environment, and reading them again before they are set is a fatal error; before, they kept the value of the previous pass. With EnergyPlus 26.2, the custom model stopped in the first timestep with "Variable = 'N' used in expression has not been initialized", in `addAccis` and in the parametric module. `ACSTnoTol` and `AHSTnoTol` are only read when the running mean outdoor temperature is within the applicability limits, which did not happen during the setup in the models checked, but could with a lower limit of 0 or less. `ComfTemp` is only read by its output variable, and the first timestep of an environment is a warmup timestep, so the results do not change.
  - `scan_zones` stops with a `ValueError` when the model has no People objects (before, `UnboundLocalError` in `setComfFieldsPeople`), with its own message when the model has `People:Definition` and `People:Instance` objects, new in EnergyPlus 26.2, which accim does not support.
  - README, Requirements (with the limitation of `People:Definition`/`People:Instance`), Detailed use, Troubleshooting, `tests/README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Files: `accim/lists.py`, `accim/sim/lists.py`, `accim/utils.py`, `accim/run/run.py`, `accim/sim/accim_Main.py`, `accim/sim/accis.py`, `accim/sim/accis_single_idf.py`, `accim/sim/accis_single_idf_funcs.py`, `accim/sim/accim_Base_EMS.py`, `accim/sim/utils.py`, `tests/core/test_energyplus_versions.py`, `tests/README.md`, `README.md`, `docs/source/1_requirements.md`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `docs/source/5_troubleshooting.md`, `accim/sample_files/jupyter_notebooks/addAccis/using_addAccis.ipynb` and `full_example_IBPSA/full_example_IBPSA.ipynb` (text of the prompt), `CHANGELOG.md`, `TODO.md`.
- Verification:
  - Transitions: the Rules and OutputChanges files of 25.2 to 26.1 and 26.1 to 26.2, and a field-by-field comparison of the three IDDs for the classes accim creates or reads (about 180). 25.2 to 26.1: only the PIU air terminals lose "Reheat Coil Air Inlet Node Name" (the resolver of `ex_*` reads outlet nodes by field name). 26.1 to 26.2: `Space` gets "Construction Assignment Set Name" before its tags and `Building` one at the end, `Material` and `Material:NoMass` get inside-face absorptances, `FenestrationSurface:Detailed` gets new surface types (accim finds windows by the names of the AirflowNetwork openings or by the ZoneVentilation objects, not by surface type), `ZoneControl:Humidistat` renames two fields and `Coil:Cooling:DX:CurveFit:OperatingMode` gets one (accim creates neither), and there are 22 new classes, among them `People:Definition` and `People:Instance`. Only some Refrigeration report variables are renamed, none read by accim.
  - `pytest tests/core`: 66 passed (46 before), in 2 minutes. The new tests of the lists, `addAccis`, `accis_single_idf_funcs.addAccis`, `runEp`, ReadVarsESO, `scan_zones` and `addEMSPCMBase` fail with the code of the previous commit (the test of the version corrected in the prompt loops there). Two tests need EnergyPlus 26.2 and skip without it: `addAccis` on the sample `TestModel` updated to 26.2, and a 1-day simulation of its custom model with EnergyPlus 26.2, which stops with the code of the previous commit ("has not been initialized"). `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 208 passed, as before.
  - Campaign with EnergyPlus 25.2 and 26.2 (scripts kept outside the repository): `TestModel_onlyGeometryForVRFsystem_2zones_CalcVent_V2510` (`vrf_ac` with ComfStand 1 and 2 and ComfMod 0 and 3; `vrf_mm` with HVACmode 1 and 2 and VentCtrl 0 and 1), `OSM_SmallOffice_exHVAC_V2320` (`ex_ac`) and `TestModel_ExistingHVAC_PTAC_V950` (`ex_mm`), updated with `update_idf_version`, `addAccis` with `'auto'`, `runEp` with `'auto'`, Seville, whole year. The 10 simulations of each version complete, with the same number of warnings and severe errors (orphaned `ZoneHVAC:IdealLoadsAirSystem` objects and warmup convergence, also in 25.2); the sums of the columns of the hourly CSV files differ by 0.003 % at most; `Table` (runperiod, building level) gives the same values, and `read_eso_using_readvarseso` finds ReadVarsESO of 26.2. With `InitASTvariables`, the results of 26.2 are the same as before it, and those of 25.2 differ by 1e-16.
  - Custom model (`ComfStand=99`): with 25.2 it completes; with 26.2 it stopped before `InitASTvariables` (with and without design days) and completes after it. aPMV setpoints (`add_vrf_system`, `apply_apmv_setpoints`): they complete with 25.2 and 26.2, with the same errors and warnings, without changes in accim. Short parametric run (`ParametricSimulation`, custom model, 4 simulations of the sample residential model, Seville, `reduce_runtime`): with 26.2 it failed before `InitASTvariables`; after it, the results differ from those of 25.2 by 0.007 % at most (cooling electricity) and those of 25.2 do not change.
  - A static check of the order of the EMS programs (variables read before any line of an earlier program sets them, in all branches) on the generated IDFs found only `m`, `n`, `ACSTnoTol` and `AHSTnoTol`, and none after the change.

The executed copies of the How-to notebooks in `docs/source/jupyter_notebooks` still show the prompt "9.1 to 25.2" until they run again. ### 2026-10-16
#### [done] Order of the hourly columns and room for the suptitles of the faceted plots; pages of tutorial 09 and wf1-wf5 run again
- Context: The entry below (pages of tutorials 00-02, 04-10 and wf1-wf3) found two issues of `accim.parametric_and_optimisation` that show on the documentation pages. `_attach_hourly_outputs_from_simulation_files` (`main.py`) collected the names of the hourly columns in a `set`, so their order changed from one Python process to the next (string hash randomisation): the list printed by `get_hourly_df_parametric` and `get_hourly_df_optimisation` (wf1, wf2, wf4, tutorial 09) and the order and colours of the series of `plot_hourly_scatter` (wf2). The suptitle of `plot_categorical_boxplots` overlapped the facet titles (tutorial 09, wf2): `subplots_adjust(top=0.9)` kept a tenth of the figure height for it, too little for one row of facets 3.6 to 4 inches high. `plot_parametric_scatter`, `plot_parametric_lines`, `plot_parametric_distributions` and `plot_parametric_ecdf` did the same (overlap in the five line plots of wf3, suptitle squeezed between the facet titles in the scatter plots of wf2, wf4 and wf5).
- Changes: a dict keeps the order of the files, or of `output_columns`. The new helper `PlottingMixin._set_grid_suptitle` adds the suptitle and calls `FacetGrid.tight_layout`, which reserves its height and keeps the room of seaborn's legend; the five methods use it, and `plot_categorical_boxplots` applies `subplots_right` after it when it draws its combined highlight legend. `tight_layout` also recomputes the other margins, so the figures with a legend outside the axes get a wider plot area: seaborn widens the figure for the legend after its own layout, which left an empty left margin that `bbox_inches='tight'` cropped (wf1 scatter: plot area 3.6 instead of 2.6 inches wide). Two entries in `CHANGELOG.md` (Fixed). Tutorial 09 and wf1-wf5 run again and exported.
- Files: `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/plotting.py`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_hourly_parametric_public_api.py`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_plotting_parametric_new_methods.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `DEVLOG.md`, and the pages of 09 and wf1-wf5 in `docs/source/jupyter_notebooks/parametric_and_optimisation_examples/`.
- Verification: 9 new tests, which fail with the old code: the order of the hourly columns from the CSV files and from `output_columns` (parametric and optimisation), the gap between the suptitle and the facet titles of the five methods, and `subplots_right` with `highlight_dict`. `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation tests/core`: 254 passed; only `test_resolve_subplot_orders_custom_missing_dimension_raises` fails, as before. Run with `docs/prepare_parametric_notebooks.py run --only 09 wf1 wf2 wf3 wf4 wf5` against `4e50053` (`git archive`, first in `PYTHONPATH`), in a new run folder with copies of the results of tutorials 02 and 07 of the entry below, so tutorial 09 reads the same results as the pages of 02 and 07: 1274 s (09 23 s, wf1 160 s, wf2 371 s, wf3 68 s, wf4 447 s, wf5 205 s), no errors. Exported with `export`, with the run folder of the entry below as a second `--executed-dir` (the results of 02 and 07 print its path): no personal paths. The pages list the hourly columns in the order of the files or of `output_columns`, and no suptitle overlaps the facet titles. Compared with the previous pages once object addresses, timestamps and BESOS folder names are masked: tutorial 09 and wf2-wf5 have the same text outputs except the lists of hourly columns (wf2, wf4), the order of the lines of parallel processes and warnings, and the part of the long logs that `export` keeps; the figures that change are those of the five methods, those of `plot_hourly_scatter` in wf2 and the points of the boxplots (random jitter). wf4 and wf5 evaluate the same solutions as before (same seed; the energy differs by at most 3e-5 J), with the rows of the optimisation in another order (parallel evaluation), so the comfort screening of wf4 gives the same best solution with another index (1 -> 7) and a few pixels of its MCDM, parallel-coordinates and m-n filter figures change. wf1 draws its sample without a seed.
- Next step: merge after PR #8.

After the split of `accim-samples`, the upload scripts uploaded every file in `dist/`, which still held `accim-0.7.8.1` (a local build of June, with the sample files, on no index). twine, sphinx-rtd-theme and myst-parser were missing from the Python 3.14 of the author; the docs script recommended `pip install -r docs/requirements.txt`, which pins numpy<2 and matplotlib<=3.7 for Read the Docs; and `sphinx-apidoc --force` overwrote a hand-written paragraph of `docs/source/api/accim.parametric_and_optimisation.rst`, a page that lacked four modules.
- Changes: New `packaging/release.py`. `tools build|upload|docs` checks the packages of each script (twine >= 6.1, which reads metadata 2.4; pandoc for nbsphinx) and offers to install the missing ones. `check` takes the files of the current version in `dist/` (files of other versions are listed and left out), checks both wheels (core: no `sample_files`, extra `samples` pinned to `accim-samples==<version>`; samples: only `accim/sample_files`, pinned to `accim==<version>`) and runs `twine check --strict`. `upload --repository pypi|testpypi [--dry-run] [--verbose]` runs the same checks, asks the index which distribution of the version is already published (skipped), warns if the version is lower than the latest, asks to type the version to confirm and uploads `accim-samples` before `accim`, with the `.pypirc` section `accim-samples` or `testpypi-accim-samples` for the samples when it is defined. The `dist_*.bat` scripts (Spanish, ASCII) call it: `dist_build_package.bat` checks the tools, cleans, builds both distributions and runs `check`; `dist_upload_test.bat` and `dist_upload.bat` check the tools and run `upload`; `dist_build_docs.bat` checks the tools, regenerates `docs/source/api`, builds the html with the warnings also in `docs/_build/sphinx_warnings.txt` (counted with `findstr`: the `find` of Git can come first in PATH), lists the changed API pages and offers to open the result. The hand-written paragraph of the API page became a link to the guide in the docstring of `filter_simulation_output_csvs`.
- Files: `packaging/release.py`, `dist_build_package.bat`, `dist_upload.bat`, `dist_upload_test.bat`, `dist_build_docs.bat`, `docs/source/api/accim.parametric_and_optimisation.rst`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Verification: twine 7.0.0, sphinx-rtd-theme 3.1.0 and myst-parser 5.1.0 installed through `tools`. `dist_build_package.bat` (40 s): core wheel 0.5 MB without `sample_files`, metadata 2.4 and `Requires-Dist: accim-samples==0.7.8; extra == "samples"`; samples wheel 29.8 MB with the 152 tracked files of `accim/sample_files`; `twine check --strict` passed for the three files. `upload --dry-run` for TestPyPI and PyPI with the 0.7.8.1 files back in `dist/`: they were left out, and `accim-samples` goes first; with a fake `.pypirc`, the section `accim-samples` was used for the samples on PyPI. Typing another version or closing the input cancels with exit code 2, also through `dist_upload_test.bat`. Nothing was uploaded. `dist_build_docs.bat` (1 min 57 s): 45 warnings, all from older docstrings and the environment; the API page documents `file_cleanup`, `hourly_plotting`, `mn_filter` and `optimisation_progress`, and the link to `HourlyPlottingMixin` in `hourly_plotting.rst` now resolves.
- Next step: first upload of `accim-samples` (TestPyPI, then PyPI), which creates the project and needs a token for the entire account.

#### [done] Fix the minor bugs found in the parametric notebooks
- Context: After the major bugs (entries of 2026-10-15), the review of the notebooks of `parametric_and_optimisation_examples` left seven minor bugs: normalisation in plots, the NSGA-II estimate, the units of two messages, the facets of the hourly plots, the units of the MCDM plot, a seaborn warning and the normalisation state after a new simulation. The author asked to fix them, each with its own test, before releasing 0.7.8.
- Changes: (1) new `PlottingMixin._is_plot_source_normalized(df_source)`, which reads the state of the plotted table (`_is_df_type_normalized`) and falls back on `outputs_normalized` for objects that do not keep it; `_normalise_plot_columns` takes `df_source` (passed by the eight `plot_parametric_*` calls), and `plot_categorical_boxplots`, `plot_best_compromise_solutions`, `plot_pareto_front` and `plot_pairwise_scatter_matrix` use it. (2) New `estimate_case_simulations(evaluations, population_size)` in `optimisation_progress.py`: the initial population plus ⌈(evaluations − population) / offspring⌉ generations of offspring, the population rounded up to an even number, as platypus NSGA-II with the BESOS operators (SBX and SSX give two children per crossover); used by `estimate_optimisation_sims`, by `preflight_report_optimisation` (same formula, same defect), by the progress of `run_optimisation` and by the header of `OptimisationProgressReporter`; docstrings updated. (3) `reduce_runtime` and `modify_timesteps` (same message) print the timesteps per hour and the maximum figures without "days". (4) `prepare_hourly_long_df` takes `extra_id_vars`, kept when they are in the hourly table; `plot_hourly_scatter` and `plot_hourly_lines` pass `row`, `col` and `hue`, and print the facets that are not in the data (`_ignore_missing_facet_columns`) instead of dropping them silently, which also happens with the default `row='CustAST_m'` and `col='CustAST_n'` on the predefined models. (5) `plot_best_compromise_solutions` converts and labels with kWh or kWh/m2 only the energy objectives (`_is_energy_like_column`), in the figure and in the printed summary. (6) `plot_categorical_boxplots` without `hue` passes `hue='Energy_Type'`, `hue_order`, `legend=False` and `dodge=False` with the palette, the replacement suggested by seaborn. (7) `run_parametric_simulation`, `_set_optimisation_outputs` (run and load of optimisations), `load_outputs_parametric` and the loads of hourly tables reset the state of the tables they replace with `_invalidate_normalized_df_types`; the tables they do not replace keep their state, so they are not divided twice.
- Files: `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/plotting.py`, `main.py`, `optimisation_progress.py`; `accim/utils.py`; `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_normalization_state.py`, `test_mcdm_plot_objective_units.py`, `test_reduce_runtime_messages.py` (new), `test_optimisation_progress.py`, `test_optimisation_preflight_report.py`, `test_plotting_hourly_methods.py`, `test_plotting_parametric_new_methods.py`; `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Verification: 25 new tests. The 21 that reproduce the bugs fail with the code of 27de193 (run against an export of that commit) and pass with the fixes; the other 4 check that the fixes go no further (a table that is not normalised is still converted, an hourly table that is not replaced keeps its state, and even populations and runs shorter than the first generation keep their count). The estimate equals the evaluations of platypus NSGA-II with populations 3, 4 and 5. `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 199 passed, 1 failed (`test_subplot_ordering.py::test_resolve_subplot_orders_custom_missing_dimension_raises`, which already failed). The boxplot without `hue` keeps the colours and layout of before (figures compared) and the seaborn FutureWarning no longer appears.
- Next step: notebook 09 no longer needs to normalise every table after building it, and wf2 no longer needs `id_vars` in `plot_hourly_scatter` (the notebooks were not changed here). `estimate_optimisation_sims` still multiplies by the number of EPWs only, not by the number of IDFs as `run_optimisation` and `preflight_report_optimisation` do (not changed).

### 2026-10-15
#### [done] Fix the plan, the extra parameters and the custom baseline of parametric runs, and MCDM per front
- Context: Running the tutorials showed that tutorial 06 did not do what it says and that tutorial 05 needed workarounds. With one IDF loaded, `run_parametric_simulation` dropped the `idf` column of the plan, so the rows meant for the low-performance model were simulated with the high-performance one. Loading both was not enough either, because `set_parameters` only wrote the custom-model baseline in the first IDF. The evaluation worker only applied accim parameters, so the roof insulation of 06 (a BESOS `FieldSelector`) never reached the model. Repeated rows of a plan were all simulated and all but the last dropped, and with several processes the results came back in completion order, which breaks Morris and Sobol. `get_best_compromise_solution` normalised and compared the fronts of all EPWs together. The author asked to fix these bugs, with tests, and to adapt tutorial 06.
- Changes: `_prepare_dataframe_for_buildings` matches the `idf` column by identifier, file name or path (`_normalize_plan_idf_value`) and raises when it names an IDF that is not loaded, with one IDF or several. `set_parameters` writes the custom baseline and the default or user values in every loaded IDF. `run_parametric_simulation` passes the BESOS parameters that are not accim parameters (`_get_additional_problem_parameters`) to the worker as a keyword argument, checks that they can be pickled when `processes > 1`, simulates each distinct task once, and returns one row per task in task order (by IDF, then EPW, then plan order). The worker gives those parameters to the BESOS problem in place of the placeholders, so BESOS writes them. `get_best_compromise_solution` applies the method to each front (`separate_by_epw`, `separate_by_idf`, defaults from the attributes of the results as in `plot_best_compromise_solutions`, which now passes its own grouping) through `_best_compromise_in_front`. Tutorial 06 loads both IDFs and simulates 2 models x 2 EPWs x 2 roof thicknesses with the same comfort model, with a table of the heating; tutorial 05 runs Morris in parallel without redrawing the plan; tutorial 10 chooses the solution of the robustness check with `get_best_compromise_solution`.
- Files: `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `analysis.py`, `plotting.py`; `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_parametric_plan.py` (new), `test_parametric_worker.py`, `test_analysis_output_resolution.py`; tutorials `05`, `06` and `10`; `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Verification: 14 new tests, 11 of which fail on the previous code. `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 174 passed, 1 failed (`test_subplot_ordering.py::test_resolve_subplot_orders_custom_missing_dimension_raises`, which already failed). Tutorials 05, 06 and 10 executed: 05 runs Morris in parallel (12 rows in plan order, 7 distinct points simulated, 64 s instead of 105 s); in 06 each row reports the planned model, the roof thickness changes the heating in the four model-climate pairs (low-performance model in Seville: 15,948 kWh with 0.04 m, 9,702 kWh with 0.16 m), and the backup of the low-performance IDF has ComfStand 99; 10 picks one TOPSIS solution per EPW and its robustness check reproduces the optimisation. With the fixes, tutorials 00-10 also run in order in a clean folder without errors (10 min), and so do wf2 and wf4 with reduced runs, whose hourly re-runs reproduce the annual HVAC electricity exactly.
- Next step: none.

#### [done] Run the tutorials 00-10 of `parametric_and_optimisation_examples` end to end
- Context: The tutorials had not been run against the current API: several failed (outputs returned as dicts, a missing `tools` module, notebook 09 creating objects with `__new__`), and others ran but gave wrong or empty results.
- Changes: in all of them, the dict results of `discover_available_outputs` and `select_outputs`, `set_output_readers` instead of the deprecated `set_outputs_for_simulation`, `reduce_runtime` with the full-year run period and no sizing periods, `PROCESSES` (half of the logical CPUs), and the final deletion behind `DELETE_RESULTS = False` (09 and 10 read the results of 02 and 07). 01: a Latin hypercube sample instead of a run before the plan existed; variable readers named after the variable and its key, averaged instead of summed; IDF count of the estimate. 02: the three EN 16798-1 categories (6 simulations) and the simulation CSV files kept for 09. 03: PMV inputs of the occupants (work efficiency 0, 0.1 m/s, seasonal clothing schedule), as in wf3. 04: non-deprecated method names. 05: the Morris analysis with one EPW, a plan drawn again until it has no repeated points, and one process (see the known issues below); Sobol is only sampled. 06: `return_time_series` from `accim.parametric_and_optimisation.objectives`. 07: even population (4) so the run matches the estimate. 08: notes on the algorithms. 09: JSON results of 02 and 07, the floor area of the sample model, and the hourly and monthly tables built before `normalize_outputs`, which then normalizes all the tables (see the known issues). 10: rewritten: the session of 07 configured again and its results loaded, compromise solutions per EPW (`plot_best_compromise_solutions`), clusters, robustness of the Seville TOPSIS solution with both EPWs, and the merge with 02.
- Files: `accim/sample_files/jupyter_notebooks/parametric_and_optimisation_examples/00_*.ipynb` to `10_*.ipynb`.
- Verification: all executed in order with nbconvert in a clean copy of the folder, without errors, in 10 min: 00 49 s, 01 69 s, 02 55 s, 03 46 s, 04 24 s, 05 105 s, 06 42 s, 07 66 s, 08 68 s, 09 21 s, 10 35 s. Notebook 10 reproduces the annual results of 07 exactly in the robustness check.
- Known issues, not fixed (they need changes in accim): with one building, `run_parametric_simulation` drops the `idf` column of the plan without a warning, so the low-performance rows of 06 were simulated with the first IDF; the worker only applies accim parameters, so the `additional_params` of besos (the roof insulation of 06) are ignored in parametric runs; `set_parameters` sets the custom-model baseline (ComfStand 99, ComfMod 3, CAT 80) only on the first building; repeated rows of a plan are simulated once and dropped, and with several processes the results come back in completion order, which breaks Morris and Sobol (05 works around both); `get_best_compromise_solution` mixes the fronts of all EPWs; `estimate_optimisation_sims` assumes `population_size` evaluations per generation, but NSGA-II creates offspring in pairs; the plotting methods check the global `outputs_normalized` flag, which stays False while any loaded table is not normalized, so a table already in kWh/m2 is divided by 3.6e6 again and labelled kWh (09 normalized only the annual table before building the hourly and monthly ones).
- Next step: the author decides whether to fix those issues in accim; then notebook 06 can load both IDFs.

#### [done] Fix the evaluation worker of `run_parametric_simulation`
- Context: While rewriting the notebooks wf2 and wf4, parametric runs failed or lost data in `_run_single_evaluation_worker`, which runs every evaluation, with one process or several: with `keep_dirs=False`, every simulation used the same `<out_dir>/BESOS_Output` folder, so parallel simulations collided and EnergyPlus failed; with several processes, two workers could try to create `BESOS_Output` at the same time (`FileExistsError`, intermittent); and the reported outputs (`add_outputs`) were looked for in the tuple returned by BESOS, which only holds the objectives and the simulation folder, so they were empty, or the first one got the folder path and `output_dir` was missing.
- Changes: the BESOS evaluator gets `out_dir=None` when `keep_dirs=False` (BESOS then runs each simulation in a temporary folder); with `keep_dirs=True`, `<out_dir>/BESOS_Output` is created with `exist_ok=True` before the evaluator; when there are reported outputs, `problem.pre_optimisation()` makes BESOS record them and the worker reads them from the last row of `problem.add_outputs_list`; `output_dir` is the last element of the BESOS result whenever `keep_dirs=True`.
- Files: `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_parametric_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Verification: the new tests call the real worker with a fake BESOS evaluator (no EnergyPlus run); 4 of the 6 fail on the previous code and all pass with the fix. `pytest tests/parametric_and_optimisation`: 160 passed, 1 failed (`test_subplot_ordering.py::test_resolve_subplot_orders_custom_missing_dimension_raises`, which already failed before the change). Notebooks wf1, wf2 and wf4 executed with reduced runs and several processes, without the notebook workarounds (see the next entry).
- Next step: none.

#### [done] Split sample files into the optional distribution `accim-samples`
- Context: More notebooks are going to be added to `accim/sample_files` (already about 125 MB uncompressed, 36 MB wheel). The user wants to install accim with or without them from pip.
- Changes: pip extras cannot add files to a distribution, only dependencies, so the sample files go into a second distribution. Root `setup.py` now excludes `accim.sample_files*` from `find_packages`, adds `exclude_package_data` as a safety net and declares `extras_require={'samples': ['accim-samples==<accim version>']}`. Root `MANIFEST.in` now uses `prune accim/sample_files`. New `packaging/accim-samples/` (setup.py, MANIFEST.in, README.md for PyPI) and `packaging/build_samples_dist.py`, which stages a copy of `accim/sample_files` (without `__pycache__`, `*.pyc`, `.ipynb_checkpoints`) in `build/samples_stage`, builds a wheel by default (`--sdist` optional) and checks its content. The samples wheel never contains `accim/__init__.py`, so both distributions install into `site-packages/accim` and uninstall independently. `accim-samples` requires `accim==<same version>`. `dist_build_package.bat` builds both; upload scripts upload both from `dist/`. `/build/` added to `.gitignore`.
- Files: `setup.py`, `MANIFEST.in`, `packaging/accim-samples/*`, `packaging/build_samples_dist.py`, `dist_build_package.bat`, `dist_upload.bat`, `dist_upload_test.bat`, `.gitignore`, `README.md`, `docs/source/1_requirements.md`, `docs/source/2_installation.md`, `docs/source/4_detailed use.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `notes/work-log.md`, `notes/decisions.md`.
- Verification: Built with Python 3.14, setuptools 82.0.1, build 1.5.0. Core wheel, built from the source tree and through the sdist: 0.51 MB, no `sample_files` entries, metadata `Provides-Extra: samples` and `Requires-Dist: accim-samples==0.7.8; extra == "samples"`. Samples wheel: 29.1 MB, 129/129 source files (same set as the filtered source tree), no `accim/__init__.py`, `Requires-Dist: accim==0.7.8`; `--sdist` also works (29.4 MB). In a temporary venv (`--no-deps`): both installed together, `import accim.sample_files` resolved to site-packages; uninstalling `accim-samples` kept accim intact, and uninstalling `accim` kept `sample_files`. Resolution of `accim[samples]` against a real index was not tested (`accim-samples` is not on PyPI yet; the name was free on 2026-10-15). The existing `dist/` (0.7.8.1) was not touched.

### 2026-10-03
#### [done] Diagnose exp 4.4 failure: EnergyPlus output path exceeds MAX_PATH
- Context: Exp 4.4 test run failed on this PC: every simulation ended with "EnergyPlus Terminated--Error(s) Detected" and no `eplusout.err` was written. The same script works on another PC.
- Root cause: `llm_project_files` is a junction to `D:\OneDrive - Universidad de Cádiz (uca.es)\...`. The script's `Path(__file__).resolve()` expanded it, so the BESOS run folder was 257 characters long. EnergyPlus 9.4 cannot open its output files at 260 or more characters (`MAX_PATH`), even with `LongPathsEnabled=1`. Reproduced: the same `in.idf` succeeds in a short path, also with "á" in it, and fails with `**FATAL:OpenOutputFiles: Could not open file ... for output` in a 262-character ASCII path.
- Changes: Added `SimulationBase._warn_if_out_dir_too_long_for_energyplus`, called from `run_optimisation` and `run_parametric_simulation`. In the 5 delivery scripts (`entrega_pc_lab_20261002/.../exp_4_*.py`), `HERE` now uses `os.path.abspath` instead of `.resolve()`. Re-applied the `run_optimisation` progress edits: most of them had not been saved to `main.py` in the previous session.

### 2026-10-03
#### [done] Add progress reporting to run_optimisation
- Context: `run_optimisation` only showed the EnergyPlus "Running…/Completed" lines from the workers. `run_parametric_simulation` already reports batch progress through tqdm.
- Changes: Added the new module `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/optimisation_progress.py`, with `OptimisationProgressReporter` and `ProgressReportingEvaluator`. The evaluator wraps `PlatypusConfig.default_evaluator`, which is `MapEvaluator` or `ProcessPoolEvaluator`. It re-implements `evaluate_all` with `submit_func` + `as_completed`, or with sequential `job.run()`, so it can report each finished job while keeping the result order. For unknown evaluators it delegates the whole batch. `run_optimisation` gained `show_progress=True` and case start/skip/end hooks. The `finally` block now always restores the original default evaluator.
- Files: `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/optimisation_progress.py`, `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_optimisation_progress.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
- Verification: New tests run Platypus NSGA-II on a toy problem with `MapEvaluator` and with `ProcessPoolEvaluator(2)`: 3 passed. `test_optimisation_checkpoint_resume.py`: 3 passed. Both runs used Python 3.14 and platypus 1.4.1. No real EnergyPlus run.
- Next step: Check the output in a real run with `processes>1` on the simulation PC (Python 3.9).


### 2026-10-03
#### [done] Add filtered copies of per-simulation output CSVs
- Context: Large campaigns need to keep only a few hourly outputs of already-run simulations. The existing `get_hourly_df*` methods build one joint DataFrame and read full CSVs, and `keep_only_outputs_in_idfs` only affects future runs.
- Changes: Added public `SimulationBase.filter_simulation_output_csvs` and private helpers. The method validates all arguments before writing. It resolves session paths with `_resolve_simulation_file_path`, including all optimisation evaluations, or accepts explicit paths, and removes duplicate paths. Columns are matched exactly, then case-insensitively, then by substring, with `Date/Time` always kept. Reads use positional `usecols`, `dtype=str` and an optional `chunksize`. Output is written through `csv.writer` with the source line terminator, so it does not depend on pandas `to_csv` keyword versions. Each file is published from a temporary file, and destinations avoid collisions. The method returns a per-file report. No existing method changed.
- Files: `accim/parametric_and_optimisation/main.py`, `tests/parametric_and_optimisation/test_filter_simulation_output_csvs.py`, `docs/source/filter_output_csvs.rst`, `docs/source/index.rst`, `docs/source/api/accim.parametric_and_optimisation.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `notes/work-log.md`.
- Verification: New tests: 33 passed. Related existing suites (`test_hourly_parametric_public_api`, `test_sim_file_cleanup`, `test_data_filtering_api`, `test_analysis_output_resolution`): 17 passed. Both runs used Python 3.14 and pandas 3.0.5. Real-data check on the Plan A results loaded from pickles, with output written to a temporary folder outside OneDrive: 4.1 wrote 120/120 files, 145.1 → 63.2 MB, and 4.2 wrote 300/300 files, 399.2 → 193.9 MB. In both runs, 4 of 9 columns were kept and a sampled copy matched the original subset verbatim. Experiment 4.3 has no consolidated results and no CSVs; it raised the expected pre-write `ValueError`. Sphinx build not run.
- Next step: Optionally run on the simulation PC with Python 3.9 / older pandas, and build the docs.
